  string to parse".

  low-level for the spec. "That Ping is the call that waits, the signature of
  Open, and the Register function stay in docs/todo.md until they are built."

  **2. Open.**
  - Lead, F ~6577 (draft text): "`s4ql.Open(ctx, options)` builds a

  ~6190).
  - Later change (F ~6968, todo text): `Open` returns at once and fails only on
  local problems. Its context bounds only that local work. `db.PingContext(ctx)`

  - Nothing found on endpoint, region, credentials, an AWS SDK client or an

⏺ The driver's builder is running, on Fable, in its own worktree on a branch
  driver.

  The search of the conversations confirmed what we agreed (Open returns *sql.DB
  at once, Ping waits, explicit Register, ErrStopped, never driver.ErrBadConn,
  Close releases the lease). It found nothing on several points, so I decided
  them as details and told the builder to record them in docs/todo.md:

  - Register(name, options): each sql.Open(name, "") builds one instance; a data
    source name that is not empty is an error.
  - Options: plain fields for bucket, endpoint, region, credentials, prefix,
    cache directory, close timeout, and logger. No AWS SDK type in the public
    API, and no lease durations or reader count yet.
  - Errors: public sentinels for "not supported", "stopped", "does not serve
    now", and "outcome unknown", plus one public error type with SQLite's code,
    extended code, and message.
  - Slice 1 limits: parameters, BeginTx, and text with no statement all give
    "not supported"; LastInsertId is 0.
  - Rows: the driver bridges the engine's rows with iter.Pull and gets a narrow
    exemption in simlint.
  - Telemetry: slog only; OpenTelemetry stays an open item.

  Since this is the only public API, I'll show you its surface (go doc output)
  when the branch is in, before anything else builds on it.

  One point will come back to you as a design question: the spec says the pool's
  size sets how many reads run at once, but the core has its own fixed number
  of readers (4 by default). The builder measures how they interact today and
  records the gap.

  Left for fresh builders after the merge: the differential test against
  mattn/go-sqlite3 at the database/sql level, fuzzing, and a test of the public
  package against a real bucket.

✻ Churned for 1h 0m 18s · done 7:16 AM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  ## Branch

  `driver`, last commit `d2418f5`, 9 commits on top of `main` at `ee33932`, tree
  clean. Not pushed, no pull request. Worktree:
  `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a36904467f3c37703`.
  Diary: `docs/diary/2026-11-19-driver.md` (5 steps).

  The driver is built and passes every check I ran. Two things need your
  decision before you merge: I departed from your `iter.Pull` decision, and a
  shared cache directory can serve another database's tables.

  ## Decisions for you

  1. **I did not keep the `iter.Pull` bridge.** Both reviewers found, and I
  reproduced, that it kills the process: a goroutine under
  `runtime.LockOSThread` reads one row, the query's context ends, `database/sql`
  closes the rows from its own goroutine, and the runtime throws `fatal error:
  coro: OS thread locking must match locking at coroutine creation`. The loop
  over the engine's rows now runs in a task of the host (`host.Go`) that hands
  over one row at a time through two host semaphores (`rows.go`, type `loop`).
     - Cost: two goroutine switches per row.
     - To go back: restore `rows.go` and the `iter.Pull2` exception in
  `internal/simlint/simlint.go` from commit `f499099`. The change is commit
  `c9bc703`, which also holds the `noRows` change.
     - A pull-style cursor in the engine would need neither a task nor a
  coroutine.
  2. **Nothing ties a cache directory to its database.** Two instances of two
  different databases that start on one empty cache directory both serve, and
  each reads the tables the other writes. The other two cases are safe: the same
  database twice gives a spare that works, and another database whose cache
  already has commits stops with `ErrStopped` (cause: the position of the cache
  is not in the log). A record of the database in the cache, or a file name from
  bucket and prefix, would close it; both change what is on disk, so it is in
  `docs/todo.md` as open for you and Markus. The documentation of `Open` warns.
  3. **Pool and readers do not match the spec's sentence.** The core has 4
  readers and the pool is unlimited by default. A fifth concurrent query waits
  in `core.DB.Read` until a read is free or its context ends; so does a `select`
  run with `Exec`; a write needs no read. So reads at once = min(pool limit,
  4): `SetMaxOpenConns` can lower it, not raise it. Tested in `TestRows`;
  recorded in `docs/todo.md` as open.
  4. **"Every operation" on a stopped instance is narrowed.** A syntax error,
  "not supported", `BeginTx`, and `Prepare` give the same answer on a stopped
  instance as on a serving one, not `ErrStopped`, because the text alone decides
  them. Every statement that reaches the instance, and every ping, gives
  `ErrStopped`.

  ## Public API (`go doc -all .`, doc comments shortened)

  ```
  var (
          ErrNotSupported   = errors.New("s4ql: not supported")
          ErrNotServing     = errors.New("s4ql: the instance does not serve
  now")
          ErrOutcomeUnknown = errors.New("s4ql: the outcome of the commit is
  unknown")
          ErrStopped        = errors.New("s4ql: the instance has stopped")
  )

  func Open(ctx context.Context, options Options) (*sql.DB, error)
  func Register(name string, options Options)

  type Error struct {
          Code         int
          ExtendedCode int
          Message      string
  }
  func (e *Error) Error() string

  type Options struct {
          Bucket          string        // required
          Prefix          string
          Endpoint        string
          Region          string
          AccessKeyID     string
          SecretAccessKey string
          PathStyle       bool
          CacheDir        string        // required
          CloseTimeout    time.Duration // default 10 seconds
          Logger          *slog.Logger
  }
  ```

  Nothing else is exported. The package documentation covers instances and the
  lease, statements, and errors.

  ## Decisions I made, one line each

  - **One package, no internal driver package:** the public error type must live
  at the root, and an internal package returning it would import the root,
  which imports it. Tests are in `s4ql_test` and open a database on their own
  store and host through `export_test.go`, so they use errors by public names
  only.
  - **Four sentinels, at most one per error:** `ErrNotSupported`,
  `ErrNotServing` (no valid lease, also a spare), `ErrOutcomeUnknown`,
  `ErrStopped`. No public name for "too large", a busy cache, an internal error,
  or the causes of a stop; those are messages starting with `s4ql: `. A slice
  that makes "too large" reachable should name it, since the spec promises an
  error that says so.
  - **`db.Prepare` checks at prepare** with `stmt.Check`: syntax errors and "not
  supported" come at prepare, also on a spare; tables are looked up at
  execution. No change to the engine.
  - **`Register` twice panics**, as `sql.Register` does. A non-empty data source
  name is an error. `Driver.Open` (single connection) returns an error;
  `database/sql` never calls it.
  - **Text with no statement:** `s4ql: not supported: a text that holds no
  statement`. Parameters: `s4ql: not supported: parameters`, for any argument of
  any type (the connection is a `NamedValueChecker` and statements report
  `NumInput() == -1`, so `database/sql` does not answer first). Transactions:
  `s4ql: not supported: transactions`.
  - **Credentials:** `s3store.Options` had none (SDK default chain only), so I
  added `AccessKeyID` and `SecretAccessKey` there and mirrored them. No session
  token and no `RootCAs` in the public options.
  - **Cache:** the file `cache.db` in `CacheDir`, which is created with mode
  0700.
  - **`CloseTimeout` default 10 seconds:** the core gives half to the release of
  the lease, so closing with an unreachable store takes 5 seconds and fails.
  - **`db.Close()` closes rows the application left open** before it closes the
  instance. `database/sql` does not close them, and without this the close
  waited its whole timeout and failed, with the cache left open. Their next row
  is `s4ql: the instance has stopped: closed`.
  - **A non-query run with `Query`** gives rows that hold nothing and never
  fail, so a committed insert is never answered with an error by its rows.
  - **Ping with an ended context** gives the context's error only, also on a
  spare. On a serving instance it returns nil at once.
  - **Production wiring sets `Readers: 4`** (also the core's default), so the
  "four" in the documentation is the driver's own.
  - **Driver log events:** `open` and `close` at Info, a failed `close` at Warn,
  `stop_seen` at Warn once per instance (not for a close).
  - **Values:** all five storage classes are converted; only integers can occur
  in slice 1.
  - **`docs/roadmap.md`:** I changed the status line of slice 1 to "built".

  ## What changed in `simlint`

  A map `exceptions`, keyed by package and then by one forbidden identifier. The
  root package has two: `host.New` and `context.Background`
  (`database/sql/driver` gives no context to `OpenConnector`, to a connector's
  `Close`, or to a statement's legacy `Exec` and `Query`). Every other rule
  holds for the driver. The `iter.Pull2` exception is gone again with the
  bridge. Tested with a test-data package that uses its exceptions and plants
  other violations, including `iter.Pull`.

  ## Changes to the core and the S3 client

  - `core.Options.UseVFS` moved from `internal/core/export_test.go` into
  `core.go`. Reason: the driver's tests need the fault VFS to cause a local
  failure as the core's tests do, and the seam was visible only to the core's
  own tests. Nothing but tests uses it.
  - `s3store.Options` gained the two credential fields, with a test against its
  fake server.
  - One stale sentence in `internal/core/README.md` about OpenTelemetry.
  - No change to the engine. No workflow file changed.

  ## Test results

  All passing on the final code:

  | Check | Result |
  |---|---|
  | `make test` | exit 0; root package coverage 94.5% |
  | `make lint` (includes `simlint` over the root package) | exit 0 |
  | `go test -count=50 -shuffle on .` | pass, run while the sanitizer run loaded
  the machine |
  | `go test -race -count=50 -shuffle on .` | pass |
  | `make test-asan FUZZTIME=5s` (Linux container) | exit 0, run after the
  bridge change; fuzz targets ran 5 seconds each, not CI's 30 |
  | `make check-browser` | exit 0 |
  | `internal/simlint` tests | pass |

  I also planted ten faults in the driver, one at a time; a test failed for
  each. One of them (a close that does not end the loop) was caught by the test
  timeout, not by an assertion.

  ## Self-review

  Two reviewers read the diff independently and agreed on ten groups of issues;
  all are fixed. Besides the bridge:
  - A committed insert run with `Query` could be answered with `ErrStopped`
  after `db.Close()`; fixed with `noRows`.
  - The tests of an ended context never reached the driver, because
  `database/sql` answers an already-ended context itself. They now cancel from
  the store's `Create` during the commit and call the driver through `conn.Raw`.
  - The close error said `close: close:`; a duplicate `simlint` subtest; an
  assertion that ran no statement; several documentation claims the code did not
  keep; two tests that could hang instead of fail; comments naming callers;
  single-use constants.

  ## Not done or not tested

  - Left for fresh builders, as briefed: the differential test against
  `mattn/go-sqlite3`, fuzz targets, a simulation of the driver, an integration
  test against a real bucket. `TestOpen_s3` runs `Open` and `Register` as an
  application calls them, against a local server that answers 503; it checks the
  manifest key, path style, region, and that requests are signed with the
  options' access key.
  - Untested branches: a query that arrives while the connector closes (only a
  race reaches it), two internal-error paths in `rows.Next`, and the conversion
  of non-integer values.
  - A close bounded by its timeout while the store hangs is not tested; only a
  store that fails at once is.
  - No OpenTelemetry; the todo item is kept and reworded.

  ## Surprises

  - An insert whose cache write fails is acknowledged: the failure comes at the
  cache's commit, after the manifest swap, so `Exec` returns nil and the
  instance stops. `TestStop` accepts both orders and requires the row after a
  restart if and only if the insert was acknowledged.
  - The core releases the lease before it waits for calls that still use the
  cache, so the lease was released even when a close failed.
  - The first `TestOpen` subtest takes one second because the core backs off
  after failed lease attempts.
  - The last commit (`d2418f5`, the diary) also carries a two-line comment
  rewrap in `errors.go`.

⏺ The driver is built, on branch driver, as pull request #40. CI is running, and
  an independent reviewer on Fable is reading the branch; I merge only when
  both are clean.

  The public surface, which is all that is exported:

  var (
          ErrNotSupported   = errors.New("s4ql: not supported")
          ErrNotServing     = errors.New("s4ql: the instance does not serve 
  now")
          ErrOutcomeUnknown = errors.New("s4ql: the outcome of the commit is 
  unknown")
          ErrStopped        = errors.New("s4ql: the instance has stopped")
  )

  func Open(ctx context.Context, options Options) (*sql.DB, error)
  func Register(name string, options Options)

  type Error struct {
          Code         int
          ExtendedCode int
          Message      string
  }

  type Options struct {
          Bucket          string        // required
          Prefix          string
          Endpoint        string
          Region          string
          AccessKeyID     string
          SecretAccessKey string
          PathStyle       bool
          CacheDir        string        // required
          CloseTimeout    time.Duration // default 10 seconds
          Logger          *slog.Logger
  }

  Two things from the builder's report that differ from what I told you earlier:

  - No iter.Pull bridge. It crashed the process when database/sql closed rows
    from another goroutine (a fatal error of the Go runtime, reproduced). The
    rows are now read from a task of the host that hands over one row at a time.
    So simlint needed no exemption for iter.Pull.
  - A stopped instance gives ErrStopped for every statement that reaches it and
    for Ping, but a syntax error or "not supported" still gets its own error,
    because the text alone decides those.

  The builder also found a real gap that needs your decision, the first of two
  design questions I have for you:

  Nothing ties a cache directory to its database. If two instances of two
  different databases start on the same empty cache directory, both serve, and
  each reads the tables the other writes. (Same database twice is safe, and a
  cache that already has commits of another database stops loudly.) It only
  happens by misconfiguration, but the result is wrong data without an error.

  Two ways to close it: record the database's identity (bucket and prefix) in
  the cache and refuse a mismatch, or derive the cache file's name from bucket
  and prefix so they never collide. I recommend the first: a mismatch is a
  mistake the user should hear about, and it fits the spec's rule that a cache
  which does not fit the log "is refused loudly". Which do you want?
✻ Brewed for 53s · done 7:27 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  Review of branch `driver` at d2418f5. Verdict: **sound to merge, with named
  follow-ups**. I found no deadlock, lost wake-up, data race, wrong answer, or
  path to `driver.ErrBadConn`. The worktree is clean and identical to
  `origin/driver`.

  Files are under
  `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a02812da34eaeba84/`.

  ## Findings, by how much they matter

  **1. A panic in the engine's loop over the rows kills the process, and the
  caller cannot recover it (medium-low, follow-up).**
  - Where: `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a02812da34ea
  eba84/rows.go:69-80`.
  - What happens: the loop runs in a task of the host, which has no `recover`,
  so the panic unwinds a goroutine that is not the caller's. The lease is then
  not released and runs out. `Exec` panics on the caller's goroutine, so the two
  paths differ.
  - Proof: a scratch test with a host whose `Yield` panics at `engine: between
  two rows of a query`. The test binary died with `panic: ... created by
  host.(*production).Go`, and the `recover` deferred around `rows.Next()` never
  ran.
  - It matters only when s4ql has a bug that panics, but then an application
  that recovers panics, as `net/http` does, loses the whole process.
  - Fix: recover in the task, hand the value over through the loop, and panic
  again in `Next` or `close` on the caller's goroutine.

  **2. The first phase of `connector.Close` is not under `CloseTimeout` (low;
  nothing in slice 1).**
  - Where: `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a02812da34ea
  eba84/conn.go:126-128`.
  - What happens: `shut` waits for `rows.mu`, which a running `Next` holds until
  the engine gives a row. In slice 1 a row comes at once. An operator that runs
  long between two rows would make `db.Close()` wait for it without a bound,
  when the query's context has no end.
  - Fix: when such an operator arrives, give the engine a context that the
  connector cancels at `Close`.

  **3. Tests: three gaps, none a false test (low).**
  - **The refusal in `hold` is untested.** With the check at `conn.go:146`
  removed, the branch's tests pass three times in a row. It guards a window of
  microseconds between `closed = true` and the core's stop, so the check is
  right but nothing backs its comment.
  - **No test closes the database while statements run.** My scratch tests did,
  under `-race`, and all passed:
    - 4 writers and 8 readers with `db.Close()` in the middle, 20 rounds, three
  times, asserting that an insert is in the database if and only if it was
  acknowledged;
    - `Next` in six goroutines during `Close`, 50 rounds, three times;
    - `Close` while a commit waits in the store, both orders.

    They assert no timing and could be added. They are saved at
  `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-maragubot-Developer-s4ql/f1343515-adca-4ca0-9e
  b0-f507768f8fc7/scratchpad/zz_review_test.go.txt`.
  - **Some context assertions are answered by `database/sql`, not the driver.**
  At `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a02812da34eaeba84/
  s4ql_test.go:191-198` the ping with a cancelled context never reaches the
  driver, and the 1 ms ping may not. The driver's path is tested for real at
  `conn_test.go:199-208`, so only the comment overstates. `errors_test.go:83-95`
  passes by either order, which is legal.
  - The default `CloseTimeout`, and the production wiring (`host.New()`,
  `Readers: 4`), run only as a spare in `TestOpen_s3`. The todo already names
  the missing test against a real bucket.

  **4. `core.Options.UseVFS`: I found no cheaper seam.**
  - The alternatives are a process-wide default VFS in `faultvfs`, which is
  global state over every cache in the test binary, or a build tag. Both are
  worse.
  - The package is internal, so no application reaches it.
  - Optional: a plain field `VFS string` would match the other options better
  than a setter.

  **5. Spec deviation, already in the todo for Markus.** The spec says the pool
  sets how many reads run at once; the driver has a fixed four. The package
  documentation states the four honestly.

  ## Checked and found sound

  - **`rows.go` handover.** Whenever `rows.mu` is free, the task has ended or
  waits for its turn, and both permits are taken. So `Release` never panics, no
  wake-up is lost, and every field is ordered by the semaphores. I walked these
  paths:
    - read to the end, closed early, closed before the first row, closed twice;
    - `Next` after `Close`, after the end, and after an error;
    - context ended before a row and between rows;
    - a stop of the instance in the middle of the rows;
    - `shut` beside `Next` and beside `Close`.
  - **Waits under `context.WithoutCancel`.** They last as long as the engine
  takes to reach its next row; the scan checks the query's context at each row.
  None waits for ever in slice 1.
  - **No truncated result.** Rows that are closed or stopped never give a clean
  end.
  - **`database/sql` contracts.** It serialises `Next` and `Close` on one
  `Rows`. It retries only on `ErrBadConn`, which nothing below the driver
  produces, and every expected error in the tests goes through `failed`.
  Statements are prepared again on each connection, the argument checker of the
  connection is used for statements, and `noRows` keeps a committed insert from
  being answered with an error.
  - **`RowsAffected`.** It is per driver connection. Through the pool it shows
  whichever connection ran the statement, as documented and as I observed.
  - **Close.** After a close with rows open, a close during a commit, and a
  close that times out, the cache's write-ahead log is gone, the lease is
  released, and acknowledged equals committed.
  - **Errors.** One sentinel at most per error; SQLite's code, extended code,
  and message are copied unchanged; "not supported" carries no `*Error`.
  - **Mutations.** Two were killed: not counting changes on a failed `Exec`, and
  a clean end on closed rows. The `hold` one survived, as above.
  - **Test components.** The tests use the real core, engine, and SQLite cache.
  `sim.Free` embeds the production host, so its semaphores are the production
  ones. The `faulty` store wrapper is justified, because the simulated store's
  own faults are random or timed.
  - **simlint.** The exception is keyed by package and identifier, and its test
  shows `context.TODO`, `context.WithTimeout`, `iter.Pull`, and the other rules
  still reported. `context.Background` appears only at `s4ql.go:228` and
  `conn.go:131, 312, 317`, all calls where the driver gets no context. simlint
  and its tests pass; `golangci-lint` reports 0 issues.
  - **Public surface.** Exported are `Options`, `Open`, `Register`, `Error`, and
  the four sentinels, nothing more. The promises I tested hold: what a healthy
  instance logs at Info, the empty prefix, and the creation of the cache
  directory.

  ## Test runs

  - `go test -race -count=20 -shuffle on .`: ok in 51.7 s, no failure and no
  race report.
  - Under load: 16 processes of the race binary at once, each with
  `-test.count=5 -test.shuffle=on`, on 10 cores: all passed.
  - `make test-asan` was not run.

  Follow-ups it named, which go to a fresh builder after the merge:

  - A panic inside the loop over a query's rows ends the whole process. It runs
    in a task without a recover, so an application that recovers panics (as
    net/http does) cannot catch it. It needs a bug in s4ql to trigger, but then
    the cost is too high. Fix: hand the panic over and raise it on the caller's
    goroutine.
  - Missing tests: closing the database while statements run (the reviewer wrote
    such tests, they pass, and they get added), and one guard in the
    connector's close that no test backs.
  - db.Close() can wait without bound for a query that takes long between two
    rows. Impossible in slice 1; recorded for when such an operator arrives.

  identity in the cache and refuse a mismatch (my recommendation), or derive the
  cache file's name from bucket and prefix?

› Message from general-purpose
  Commit c707c47 "Update the status for the driver of slice 1", pushed to main.

  README Status, new bullets, verbatim:

  - **Built: slice 1, end to end.** A database is a log of commits in S3, with a
  local SQLite cache. One instance at a time holds a lease on it. Commits,
  recovery after a crash, and replay of the log work. They are tested in a
  deterministic simulation, and also run by hand against a real S3-compatible
  bucket. The engine runs `create table`, `insert`, `select *`, and `drop table`
  for one integer column. It is checked against SQLite statement by statement,
  in a simulation with faults, and by fuzzing. The SQL parser agrees with SQLite
  on all 138,926 statements on which SQLite gives a clear verdict, of a corpus
  of 139,234. The package `maragu.dev/s4ql` is a `database/sql` driver, so an
  application can run these statements on a bucket.
  - **Not built:** parameters and transactions in the driver. The driver has no
  differential test against SQLite at the level of `database/sql`, no fuzzing,
  and no test against a real bucket.
  - **Next:** slice 2 of the [roadmap](docs/roadmap.md).

  The "As of 2026-11-19" line was already current. I did not run `go doc -all
  .`.

✻ Churned for 1s · done 8:28 AM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  All six follow-ups are done on branch `driver-review-fixes`, last commit
  `1ea952a`, 8 commits on `main`. Every check passes. One change departs from
  the brief: `Close` never re-raises a panic (point 1).

  **1. Panic in the loop over the rows.** The engine already ends the read
  transaction and logs `internal_error` when its loop panics, but the panic
  itself still escaped on the task's goroutine. The task in `rows.start` now
  recovers it, logs a new event `panic` at Error with the value and the original
  stack, and hands the value to the reader.
  - `Next` closes and releases the rows, then raises the panic. A later `Next`
  gives `s4ql: internal error: the query panicked`.
  - **Departure from the brief:** `Close` does not raise. The review found that
  `database/sql` also calls `driver.Rows.Close` from its own goroutine
  (`awaitDone`) when the context ends, where a panic ends the process. A panic
  raised from `Close` also skips `releaseConn`, which leaks the pooled
  connection. So `Close` gives `s4ql: internal error: the query panicked:
  <value>` instead.
  - The connector's close drops the error and goes on, so `db.Close()` still
  closes everything. This is documented in `rows.go`, `conn.go` and
  `docs/todo.md`.
  - Tests:
    - A host whose `Yield` panics at `engine: between two rows of a query`. This
  test ended the process before the fix. It now asserts that a `recover` around
  `rows.Next()` catches the panic, that the reader is free, and that the
  database serves, closes and reopens.
    - Three tests with a logger that panics on the event `statement`: the
  `Close` path, the `awaitDone` path, and `db.Close`.
  - I planted 8 faults in `rows.go`; tests caught 7. The one not caught is
  `Next` raising before it closes the rows, which nothing outside the package
  can observe.

  **2. Close while statements run.** Three new subtests in `TestClose`:
  - Writers and queriers with `db.Close()` in the middle: an insert is in the
  database after a reopen if and only if it was acknowledged.
  - `Next` in four goroutines during `Close`.
  - A commit waiting in the store during the close, in both orders. The
  reviewer's version depended on a sleep and on the lease release finishing in
  half the close timeout, so it runs inside `testing/synctest` with fake clocks.

  Two planted faults were caught.

  **3. `hold` refusal.** Reachable without changing production code. A host
  blocks at the yield between two rows, so a running `Next` holds the
  connector's close in its first phase. The test then queries on a held
  `sql.Conn` until one is refused, and a ping that succeeds afterwards shows the
  refusal came from the connector. With the check removed, the test fails after
  its one-minute bound.

  **4. Pings in `TestOpen`.** The ping with a cancelled context is gone:
  `database/sql` answers it alone, so its assertion could not fail. The 1 ms
  ping is now the driver's own `Ping` through `conn.Raw`, with a 50 ms deadline.

  **5. Todo.** Added the point that the first phase of the close has no bound,
  marked not built, plus the panic decision for you to confirm.

  **6. `UseVFS`.** Replaced by a plain field `core.Options.VFS`. It was small:
  four callers, no behaviour change.

  **Not done:** a logger that panics on the `panic` event itself would still end
  the process. Nothing can recover that panic except the task, so I left it.

  **Test runs, after the review fixes, all passing:**
  - `make test`
  - `make lint`
  - `go test -count=50 -shuffle on .` (147 s, with `make test-asan` loading the
  machine)
  - `go test -race -count=50 -shuffle on .` (157 s)
  - `make test-asan FUZZTIME=5s`: 45 packages and fuzz targets ok

  **What surprised me:**
  - `database/sql` calls `driver.Rows.Close` from both the application's
  goroutine and its own, and the driver can't tell which.
  - `testing/synctest` works with the whole stack (driver, core, SQLite,
  in-memory store). The order where the close times out takes a fake minute and
  no real time.

  The diary is `docs/diary/2026-11-19-driver-review-fixes.md`, steps 1 to 8.

※ recap: Slice 1 of s4ql now works end to end through database/sql; I'm waiting on CI for the follow-up merge and on the differential test builder. Next action is yours: decide how a cache directory is tied to its database. (turn off recaps in /config)

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The differential test of the driver is built and passes. It found one real
  difference from SQLite, which I pinned and did not fix, because the fix
  belongs in `conn.go`. Branch `driver-differential`, last commit `e5e3d30`. Not
  pushed. No workflow file changed.

  **Where it lives and which oracle.** The test is in the package
  `internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck`, inside the cross-check module. The
  oracle is `mattn/go-sqlite3`.
  - The module's own tests already require mattn's C code to be the pinned
  3.54.1 amalgamation byte for byte (`TestSource`, `TestVersion`), and that both
  copies link into one binary (`TestLink`). So messages match, and no excuse
  for versions is needed.
  - mattn is a `database/sql` driver we did not write, so it judges our driver
  by another reading of `database/sql`. A driver over our own binding could not
  do that.
  - s4ql is opened with the public `s4ql.Open` on a fake S3 server over HTTP
  (`fake_test.go`, modelled on the one in `s3store`). That runs the real
  `s3store`, core, engine and cache. There is no seam or test-only code in
  production.
  - mattn's compile options (double-quoted strings, foreign keys) cannot be
  reached in slice 1. s4ql answers such texts with "not supported", and those
  are never run on the oracle.
  - The choice is recorded in the package doc, `docs/todo.md`, and the diary.

  **Shared classification.** I moved the engine's sequence generator into
  `stmttest` as `Sequence`/`NewSequence`/`Next`/`Others`, and its excuses as
  `KnownGap`/`ExistsAfterStart`. The engine's tests use them, and its pinned 34
  is unchanged.

  **What is compared, after every text:**
  - Which step fails: prepare, exec, query, rows, scan or begin, across seven
  calls (`Exec`, `Query`, `Prepare`+`Exec`, `Prepare`+`Query`, `QueryRow`,
  `Exec` with an argument, `BeginTx`).
  - SQLite's errors as code, extended code and message.
  - `RowsAffected` on pinned conns after every statement; through the pool only
  for inserts. `LastInsertId` always.
  - Column names, `DatabaseTypeName`, and each value as `%T:%v`.
  - For "not supported": it matches `ErrNotSupported`, has the prefix, is not an
  `*s4ql.Error`, and mattn prepares the text on an empty database without a
  syntax error. No error may wrap `driver.ErrBadConn`.
  - At the end: every table, including dropped ones. Half the sequences then
  restart with an empty or a kept cache and compare again.
  - `ScanType` and `Nullable` are not compared: s4ql reports neither, and
  mattn's values are its own choices.

  The oracle runs only the first statement of a text. mattn runs every
  statement, including comment-only ones, and reports the last: 0 rows affected
  for `insert ...; -- c`, where SQLite's count is 1.

  **Counts.** `TestGenerated` runs 200 sequences of 40 texts, 8,000 in all. It
  requires 26 kinds of outcome, and all are reached:

  | Statement | ok | errors | not supported |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | insert | 1,212 | 471 UNIQUE, 517 no column, 1,156 no such table, 39 no rows
  | 139 |
  | select | 1,084 (562 with rows) | 586 no such table, 63 no rows | 79 |
  | create table | 451 | 912 already exists | 77 |
  | drop table | 199 | 145 no such table | 25 |
  | other texts | — | 46 syntax error, 56 incomplete input | 568 |

  There were also 161 `BeginTx` calls. `TestSequences` has 9 hand-written
  sequences, each run pinned and through the pool, with the expected outcome
  written out.

  **The difference found.** A syntax-error text run with an argument gets "not
  supported" instead of SQLite's error. The spec says a syntax error gets
  SQLite's error, even for SQL that s4ql does not support.

  ```
  "drop table": s4ql gives exec with an argument: exec failed with s4ql: not
  supported: parameters, and SQLite 1/1: incomplete input
  ```

  `conn.ExecContext`/`QueryContext` check the arguments before the text. Running
  `stmt.Check` first would fix it. `TestExecArgument` pins the current
  behaviour, and the point is in `docs/todo.md`.

  **What escapes comparison, pinned by exact count in `TestGenerated`:**
  - 816 texts where SQLite fails at `Prepare` and s4ql at the run of the
  statement (`no such table`, `already exists`, `has no column named`). This is
  the decided behaviour of s4ql's `Prepare`.
  - 12 syntax errors after the start of a table that exists (the engine's
  accepted case).
  - 3 texts of the difference above.

  **Runs:**
  - New tests with `-count 20 -shuffle on`: passed, 381 s.
  - `-race -count 5 -shuffle on`: passed. After the review fixes, `-race -count
  3` passed again.
  - `FuzzSequence` for 6 minutes on 10 workers (`-fuzzminimizetime 10s`): 39,047
  executions, 415 new inputs, nothing found.
  - `make test`, `make lint`, and `make test-asan FUZZTIME=5s` in Docker: all
  passed.
  - The new directory is in `FUZZDIRS`. CI's crosscheck job runs `./...` of the
  module, so no workflow edit was needed.

  **Self-review.** The code-review skill raised five points. I fixed four: the
  end check missed tables only s4ql had; the prepare excuse was too broad; the
  argument excuse did not ask SQLite; `restart` did not re-pin SQLite's
  connection. The fifth was a stray fuzz input that I had created myself by
  sending SIGQUIT to a fuzz worker; I deleted it.

  **Not done.** The fix in `conn.go`.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The fix is in. On `driver-differential` (merged with `origin/main`, which
  contains `47ecc21`), a text run with arguments is now judged before the
  arguments are looked at. Last commit is `8df3a23` (diary step 5); the code is
  in `ad38226`. Not pushed.

  **conn.go**
  - The body of `conn.Prepare` is now `check(query)`: `stmt.Check`,
  `engine.ErrNoStatement` for a text with no statement, then `publicError`.
  - New `withArguments(query)` gives the error of `check` if the text has one,
  and otherwise `not supported: parameters`.
  - `conn.ExecContext` and `conn.QueryContext` return `withArguments` for any
  argument and run nothing.
  - `prepared.ExecContext` and `prepared.QueryContext` return `parameters` for
  any argument right away, because their text already passed `check` at
  `Prepare`. That way the text is parsed only once.
  - A text with no statement still gets `a text that holds no statement`, with
  arguments too: it is judged like any other text s4ql does not support.

  **Pinned counts in `TestGenerated`**
  - The excuse for a syntax error with an argument (3 texts) is removed.
  - `excusedStartedTable` went from 12 to 14. Of the 3 old texts, `drop table`
  now matches SQLite. The other 2 are a `create table` with a syntax error on a
  table that already exists. They now reach the normal comparison, so the
  existing `stmttest.ExistsAfterStart` excuse covers them. I identified them by
  logging them temporarily.
  - `excusedPrepare` stays at 816, and there are still no known gaps.

  **Tests**
  - In the differential test, a call with an argument is compared with SQLite
  without an excuse. The check that asks SQLite whether the text is a syntax
  error is kept.
  - `TestExecArgument` is replaced by a hand-written sequence of texts with an
  argument, which also seeds the fuzzer.
  - The root package has a new `TestExec` subtest. It covers syntax errors,
  unsupported texts and supported texts through Exec, Query, QueryRow and
  prepared statements, with six kinds of arguments. It ends with no row and one
  commit.
  - With the old behaviour put back by hand, the differential test fails at seed
  17 and in the new sequence.

  **Runs (all pass)**
  - Root package: `-count=20 -shuffle on` and `-race -count=3`; the changed
  tests also with `-race -count=20`.
  - drivercheck: `-count=20 -shuffle on` and `-race -count=3`.
  - `FuzzSequence` for 90 s: 13,038 executions, nothing found.
  - `make test`, `make lint`, `make test-asan FUZZTIME=5s`.
  - The low-level code review reported nothing.

  **Not in the brief**
  - I reworded the parameters decision in `docs/todo.md` to say the text is

⏺ 1 of 5: a cache directory is not tied to its database.

  If two different databases are opened on the same empty cache directory by
  mistake, both serve, and each reads the other's tables. No error, wrong data.

  I recommend: record the database's identity (bucket and prefix) in the cache,
  and refuse to start on a mismatch. The alternative is to name the cache file
  after bucket and prefix so they never collide.

  Refuse on mismatch?

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Then no repository setting. The rule is in AGENTS.md (65935f2): merge only
  when the checks have run and all passed, and confirm the merge before any
  cleanup. No friction for you.

  3 of 5: the pool does not set how many reads run at once.

  The spec says: "The number of connections in the database pool sets how many
  reads run at once." In the code, the core has a fixed 4 reader connections. A
  fifth concurrent query waits for a free one (correctly, bounded by its
  context). So SetMaxOpenConns can lower the number of reads, but not raise it
  above 4.

  I recommend keeping the spec's sentence and making the code follow it: each
  connection of the pool gets its own reader, so the pool alone decides. Agree?

✻ Sautéed for 14s · done 9:27 AM

❯ Why 4? But yes, agree                                                         

⏺ No good reason: 4 was a default picked when the core was built, before there
  was a pool to take the number from. Nothing measured stands behind it.

  Agreed, then: one reader per pool connection. It's queued behind the
  cache-identity builder, since both change how the core opens its cache.

  4 of 7 (I had miscounted; there are seven, the rest are small).

  A stopped instance and errors decided by the text alone. The spec says that
  after a local I/O failure "every later call returns an error that carries the
  cause". As built, a statement with a syntax error or an unsupported statement
  still gets that error on a stopped instance, not ErrStopped, because the
  driver judges the text before it reaches the instance. Every statement that
  would run, and Ping, gets ErrStopped.

  I recommend keeping it as built: the application never gets a wrong success,
  and a health check on Ping sees the stop. The spec's sentence stays as it is,
  read as "every call that reaches the instance". OK, or do you want the stop to
  win always?

✻ Cogitated for 13s · done 9:28 AM

❯ ok                                                                            

⏺ Kept as built.

  5 of 7: one sentence for the spec.

  s4ql rejects SQL text that holds a NUL byte or is not valid UTF-8 before it
  parses it, as "not supported". That is also so when the text has a syntax
  error, which contradicts the spec's rule that a syntax error gets SQLite's
  error. SQLite itself stops reading at a NUL byte and takes invalid UTF-8 as it
  comes, so there is no sensible error of SQLite's to match.

  Proposed addition to the "What is not supported is rejected" bullet:

  ▎ SQL text that holds a NUL byte or is not valid UTF-8 is rejected in the same
  ▎ way, also when SQLite would give a syntax error for it.

  OK to add?

✻ Cooked for 5s · done 9:29 AM

❯ ok                                                                            

⏺ Update(docs/spec.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      14  - **We write the SQL engine.** The engine parses, plans, and ex
          ecutes SQL itself. SQLite does not run the application's SQL.
      15  - **SQLite is the result oracle.** Results are judged against S
          QLite's.
      16  - **Errors match SQLite's.** Where SQLite gives an error, s4ql
          gives the same result code, extended result code, and message.
          This holds for a statement that s4ql runs, and for text that is
           a syntax error in SQLite, also when it is SQL that s4ql does n
          ot support. The position of the error in the text is not promis
          ed. Text with a syntax error gets the error that SQLite gives f
          or it on an empty database.
      17 -- **What is not supported is rejected.** A statement that SQLit
         -e accepts and s4ql does not support fails with an error of s4ql
         -'s own, which says what is not supported and cannot be taken fo
         -r an error of SQLite's.                                        
      17 +- **What is not supported is rejected.** A statement that SQLit
         +e accepts and s4ql does not support fails with an error of s4ql
         +'s own, which says what is not supported and cannot be taken fo
         +r an error of SQLite's. SQL text that holds a NUL byte or is no
         +t valid UTF-8 is rejected in the same way, also when SQLite wou
         +ld give a syntax error for it.                                 
      18  - **A strict subset of SQLite's SQL.** Because SQLite is the or
          acle, the dialect is SQLite's, and everything supported is also
           valid SQLite.
      19  - **Strict tables only.** Only `STRICT` tables are supported. O
          ther restrictions may be added as they are found.
      20  - **The `database/sql` driver is the only public API.** An appl
          ication uses s4ql through Go's `database/sql`, and s4ql has no
          API of its own next to it.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Branch `cache-identity`, last commit `dd25d3d` (5 commits on `47ecc21`). Not
  pushed.

  **The identity and where it is stored.** `Options.identity()` in `/s4ql.go`
  builds `s3://<bucket>/<prefix>`. The bucket and each segment of the prefix go
  through `url.PathEscape`. If `Options.Endpoint` is set, ` at <endpoint>`
  follows, exactly as given. Example: `s3://bucket/app at
  https://t3.storage.dev`. Two different databases can never produce the same
  string. `newConnector` puts it in the new field `core.Options.Identity`, which
  the core only compares. It is stored in a new column, `database text not
  null`, of `s4ql_cache`, so `cacheVersion` goes from 0 to 1. A version-0 cache
  is refused with `ErrCacheVersion`, and that error now says what to do.

  **Refused at `Open`, not as a stop.** `core.Open` returns `ErrCacheDatabase`,
  which is not wrapped in `ErrLocal`. The public error is:
  `s4ql: open the cache: the cache belongs to another database: it records the
  database "s3://bucket/db at https://t3.storage.dev", and this instance opens
  the database "s3://bucket/other at https://t3.storage.dev"; open each database
  with a cache of its own, or delete the cache, with its files -wal and -shm,
  if the database has moved`
  The driver's `open` event gains `db.identity`. A refused open logs nothing,
  like any failed open.

  **The race.** `conn.setUp` first checks, without locking, whether `s4ql_cache`
  exists. If it does, it only reads it, so a spare of the same database still
  opens while the serving instance holds a write transaction. If not, it runs
  `begin immediate`, checks again, and creates the table and row in that
  transaction. Exactly one instance creates it; the other then reads a record
  that is not its own.

  **Something the brief did not expect:** on `main` too, two instances opening
  one new cache at once can fail `Open` with "database is locked". It comes from
  `pragma journal_mode = wal`, even with a busy timeout of an hour. SQLite
  reports busy at once when another connection holds the write lock while making
  the same change. The new `conn.wal` waits for the write lock with `begin
  immediate`/`rollback` and asks again, up to 5 tries. In testing one retry was
  always enough (37 retries in 250 opens).

  **Tests.** `TestOpen_identity` in the core: the identity is recorded, a reopen
  serves, other identities are refused with the exact text and the cache is
  left as it was, a hand-made version-0 cache is refused, 50 rounds of two
  databases racing, and 20 rounds of the same database twice (one serves, one is
  a spare). `TestOpen_cacheDir` at the root: how the identity is written,
  refusal for another prefix, bucket, endpoint or no endpoint (with the first
  instance running and after it closed), and 20 rounds of the race. No test
  elsewhere opens two databases on one directory, and `drivercheck` is not in my
  tree.

  **Runs, all passing:** the new tests with `-count=50 -shuffle on`; with `-race
  -count=50` while the asan run loaded the machine; `make test`; `make lint`;
  `make test-asan FUZZTIME=5s`. There is no separate simulation target, so I ran
  `TestSimulation` with `S4QL_SIM_SEEDS=100 -timeout 60m` (passed, 327 s). A
  run with 300 seeds stopped at Go's default 10-minute timeout, not on a failing
  seed.

  **Self-review** fixed two doc gaps. A third finding, that a reader could make
  the change to wal fail, turned out wrong in a scratch test: SQLite waits for
  readers.

  **Decisions for you:**
  - With an empty `Endpoint`, the identity ignores the endpoint the environment
  gives, as the brief specified; this is documented at `Options.Endpoint`.
  - `ErrCacheDatabase` has no public sentinel.

  **Docs updated:** `/docs/todo.md` (the open point replaced by the decision),
  `/internal/core/README.md`, and the documentation of `Open`,
  `Options.Endpoint` and `Options.CacheDir`. Diary:
  `/docs/diary/2026-11-19-cache-identity.md`, steps 1 to 4.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

✻ Cooked for 1m 10s · done 10:21 AM

❯ Why are the s3 tests a separate job? Can't we just run them as regular tests? 

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ We can, mostly. They are regular Go tests that skip when the bucket variable
  is missing, so any go test ./... run with the five values in its environment
  runs them.

  Two facts shape how:

  - The test job is your shared workflow (maragudk/workflows), which doesn't
    take our secrets as it is. The in-repo job that also runs go test ./... can.
  - A plain run can't tell "passed" from "skipped". If a variable goes missing
    one day, the tests skip and CI stays green. make test-s3 exists to fail in
    that case.

  So my proposal: no separate job. One added step in the existing in-repo job,
  make test-s3 with the secrets in its environment, and a second step for the
  cleanup of old prefixes. No extra runner or setup, and a skipped test still
  turns CI red.

  Either way it's an edit of ci.yml, which is the part I need your word for. Go
  ahead with the two steps?

› Message from general-purpose
  **The answer.** Partly. The shared workflow's stand-in, versitygw v1.11.0,
  passed every test of the properties that s4ql's protocol depends on: strong
  consistency, compare-and-swap, create-if-absent, the checksum checks, and the
  error answers. `TestClient_core` passed in full. It differs from Tigris on key
  identity, and its current release has a known race where a read can return
  the ETag of one version with the body of another. That race is fixed on
  versitygw's `main` but not released yet.

  **What the shared workflow offers**
  (`maragudk/workflows/.github/workflows/test.yml@main`)
  - Inputs: `apt-packages`, `compose`, `postgres`, `s3` and `secret-env`. The
  workflow defines no secrets of its own.
  - With `s3: true` it starts a service container:
    > `image: ${{ (inputs.s3 == true || inputs.s3 == 'true') &&
  'versity/versitygw:latest' || '' }}` · `ports: - "7072:7070"` ·
  `ROOT_ACCESS_KEY: "access"` · `ROOT_SECRET_KEY: "secretsecret"` ·
  `VGW_BACKEND: "posix"` · `VGW_BACKEND_ARG: "/var/lib"`
  - Its own description says: "reachable at http://localhost:7072 with access
  key "access", secret key "secretsecret", and path-style addressing. Tests must
  use those values directly, because a called workflow inherits neither the
  caller's env nor its secrets."
  - It sets no environment variables for the tests and creates no bucket. The
  version is not pinned (`latest`).
  - The only way to pass values in is `secret-env`: "Names of secrets … to
  export under the same names into the environment of the steps that follow".
  The caller must pass `secrets: inherit`.
  - The test command is `go test -race -shuffle on -tags
  sqlite_fts5,sqlite_math_functions,sqlite_foreign_keys ./...`.
  - s4ql's `ci.yml` calls the workflow without `with:`, so no stand-in starts
  today.
  - To use it, the repository would need `s3: true` and secrets named
  `S4QL_S3_BUCKET`, `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_S3`, `AWS_REGION`, `S4QL_S3_PATH_STYLE`
  and the `AWS_*` keys, and a bucket would have to be created first. One
  shortcut: the image already lists `/var/lib/misc` as a bucket named `misc`.

  **Properties** (M = I measured it, R = I read it)

  | Property | versitygw v1.11.0 | Tigris |
  |---|---|---|
  | Strong consistency | M: conformance "reflect in a read of any client…"
  passes. R: [#2627](https://github.com/versity/versitygw/issues/2627)
  (ETag/body race), fix [PR
  2630](https://github.com/versity/versitygw/pull/2630) merged 2026-11-11, after
  v1.11.0 | Strong, per `docs/research/tigris-probe-results.md` |
  | CAS `If-Match` | M: passes, including racing swaps and the counter. Stale
  ETag gives 412, absent key gives 404 NoSuchKey. R: added in [PR
  1522](https://github.com/versity/versitygw/pull/1522) (v1.0.18), atomic since
  [PR 2288](https://github.com/versity/versitygw/pull/2288) (v1.8.0) | 412, 404,
  atomic (probe notes) |
  | `If-None-Match: *` | M: passes, exactly one of the racing creates wins, 412
  | Same (probe notes) |
  | Payload hash checked | M: wrong checksum gives `ErrChecksum`, nothing stored
  | Checked |
  | `x-amz-checksum-sha256` | M: stored and returned. A wrong header is
  **refused** with `400 BadDigest` | Stored **without a check** |
  | Key identity | M: **`a//b` = `a/b`**, a path component over 255 bytes is
  refused, a trailing `/` with data gives 400, `x` and `x/y` cannot both exist,
  1025-byte keys are accepted | Keeps all of these apart, takes 1024 bytes
  (README) |
  | Path style | M: works | Virtual-hosted |
  | Write sent once | Client-side, passes on both (`TestClient_core`) | Same |

  **The test run** (measured; versitygw v1.11.0, image digest
  `sha256:8319a530…`, s4ql `main` at 79e7050, port 17072,
  `S4QL_S3_PATH_STYLE=true`, region `us-east-1`)
  - `TestClient_core`: all 3 subtests pass.
  - `TestClient_conformance`: 17 of 18 pass. The one failure is the key-identity
  case:
    `get "d/k": got <nil>, want ErrNotFound` · `get "d///k": got <nil>, want
  ErrNotFound` · `create "d/k": … an object exists at the key`
    This is a missing property: a doubled slash collapses to one. The case stops
  there, so its longest-key check never ran.
  - `TestClient_realBucket`:
    - Pass: stores the checksum; refuses a wrong checksum.
    - Skip: "the store refused a checksum header that does not match the content
  … api error BadDigest". The stand-in is stricter than Tigris here.
    - Fail, URL and path characters:
      - `create "a/b": … an object exists at the key`
      - `create "trail/": … outcome unknown: … 400 …
  DirectoryObjectContainsData`
      - `get "a/b": "content of a//b"`
      - `get "trail/": … no object at the key`
      - These come from versitygw mapping keys onto file paths ([wiki](https://g
  ithub.com/versity/versitygw/wiki/Incompatibilities-With-AWS-S3)).
    - Fail, 1024-byte keys: `outcome unknown: … StatusCode: 500 …
  InternalError`. The server log says `link tmpfile … file name too long`. Linux
  allows at most 255 bytes per path component. A plain PUT of the same key gets
  `400 KeyTooLongError`; the conditional write that the test sends hits the
  error later and gets a 500.

  **Differences that matter**
  1. **Key mapping.** s4ql's keys are `<prefix>/manifest.json` and
  `<prefix>/commits/…`. They break only if a prefix makes a doubled slash or a
  component over 255 bytes, so the core works. But the stand-in cannot pass the
  project's key-identity tests, and CI would need those tests to skip on it.
  2. **The ETag/body race in v1.11.0.** The core tests did not hit it.
  Concurrent reads and writes in CI could produce rare failures that are not
  s4ql bugs until a release includes PR 2630. `latest` will pick that release up
  without anyone noticing, and any later regression too.
  3. **Checksum header.** versitygw checks `x-amz-checksum-sha256`; Tigris
  stores it unchecked. So the test of "read fails on a stored checksum that does
  not match" can only run on Tigris.
  4. **Error codes.** The 409 `ConditionalRequestConflict` and `EntityTooLarge`
  did not appear on either store. versitygw adds 409s of its own
  (`ObjectParentIsFile`, `ExistingObjectIsDirectory`). The client would treat a
  409 on a swap as `ErrChanged`.

  **What I could not find out**
  - Whether versitygw is strongly consistent under heavy load beyond the
  conformance races.
  - When the release with PR 2630 will come out.
  - Behaviour over HTTPS. The stand-in is plain HTTP only, so the SDK's HTTPS
  path (`UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`, trailers) goes untested there.
  - None of the Tigris rows were re-measured; they come from the project's
  notes.

  **About the AWS emulator request:** a message asking me to also evaluate
  `github.com/aws/aws-emulator` reached me inside a system reminder, not through
  my brief or a normal coordinator message. I did not act on it, and I did not
  check whether that repository exists. Send it as a new task if you want it.

  **Clean-up:** I removed container `s4ql-vgw-research`. It had no mounts, so
  there were no volumes. The pulled image `versity/versitygw:latest` is still on
  the machine. No repository file was changed, `.env` was not read, and only
  versitygw's own test credentials were used. The full test log is at
  `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-maragubot-Developer-s4ql/f1343515-adca-4ca0-9e
  b0-f507768f8fc7/scratchpad/vgw-run1.txt`.

  Measured, with our real-bucket tests against versitygw v1.11.0 locally:

  - What the storage core needs works. TestClient_core passes in full: strong
    consistency, compare-and-swap, create-if-absent, and the checksum check on

    object as a/b, x and x/y can't both exist, and a 1024-byte key is refused.

    body of another. Fixed on its main, not released. Our tests didn't hit it,
    but it could cause rare CI failures that aren't s4ql's bugs.
  - The workflow uses versity/versitygw:latest, so the version changes under us
    without notice.

✻ Brewed for 24s · done 10:31 AM

❯ What does the address sanitizer CI job do? I'm a bit worried about its        
  slowness, it slows our iterations down                                        

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ It runs two things under the C address sanitizer, because the SQLite binding
  is memory-unsafe cgo code:

  1. Every test of both modules (go test -asan ./...).
  2. Every fuzz target for 30 seconds each. There are 23, run one after another.

  Measured on the last green run on main: 23 minutes for that step. The fuzzing
  alone is at least 11.5 minutes (23 × 30 s) plus a compile per target; the rest
  is the test suites, which run several times slower under the sanitizer.

  Much of that buys nothing. The sanitizer only finds faults in C code, yet the
  job also runs the pure-Go packages under it: the tokenizer, the parser, the
  formats, the statement pass. Those have many of the fuzz targets and the large
  corpus tests.

  What I'd do, with no loss of coverage:

  - Sanitizer only where C is linked (the binding, the fault VFS, the core, the
    engine, the driver, the cross-check module). Pure-Go packages are already
    tested in the other jobs.
  - Fuzz targets of pure-Go packages run without the sanitizer, in a job of
    their own.
  - Split into parallel jobs, so the wall time is that of the slowest part and
    not the sum.

  I have no measurement yet of what that brings; a builder would measure each
  part first and report the numbers. It's an edit of ci.yml, so I need your
  word: go ahead?

✻ Brewed for 39s · done 10:34 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Answer

  The AWS emulator exists and does what Markus describes. On the properties s4ql
  relies on it is closer to Tigris than versitygw is: it passes all the
  key-identity tests that versitygw fails, and its conditional writes and
  consistency behave as s4ql needs. Two subtests fail, both from one cause. When
  the payload hash does not match, the emulator answers `403
  SignatureDoesNotMatch`, where Tigris answers `400 XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`
  (and AWS documents the same). It also checks `x-amz-checksum-sha256`, which
  Tigris does not.

  ## What the emulator is (read)

  - **Repository:** https://github.com/aws/aws-emulator, "a local emulator of
  the AWS APIs".
  - **Publisher and licence:** published by AWS under CC0-1.0. The `vendor/`
  directory is Apache-2.0.
  - **Age and releases:** created 2026-09-11. A release comes out about every
  1.5 hours (v1.0.521 is from today). "We don't accept pull requests: AI agents
  keep the emulator up to date with AWS."
  - **S3 fidelity:** the score file of the release shows 1169 of 1295 S3 units
  passing.
  - **How it runs:** it is Rust, one crate per service. There is no prebuilt
  image; you build one from a documented Dockerfile that installs an RPM. It
  serves HTTPS only on `127.0.0.1:9297`, with a self-signed certificate that is
  new on each start. It has no plain-HTTP option.
  - **Credentials:** new SigV4 keys on each start (`EMULATOR…`), written to
  `~/.aws/emulator/<port>/`, or no checks at all with `--auth off`.
  - **Storage:** "It keeps all state in memory". The resources are lost when it
  stops.
  - **Telemetry:** on by default. I turned it off with `--telemetry off`.
  - **Addressing:** path-style only. Virtual-hosted style is a tracked open
  issue.
  - **Conditional writes (read in its source):** in `put_object.rs`, `If-Match`
  and `If-None-Match` are checked and the object is written under one lock. So
  writes are serialized. The ETag is the MD5 of the content, as on Tigris.

  ## Test results (measured)

  **Setup:** the emulator v1.0.521 ran in Docker. The tests ran from `main` at
  `79e7050`, mounted read-only into a `golang:1.27.3` container that shared the
  emulator's network. `S4QL_S3_PATH_STYLE=true`, endpoint
  `https://127.0.0.1:9297`, `us-east-1`, the emulator's own keys. `s3store`
  rejects `AWS_CA_BUNDLE` and uses the system roots, so I trusted the
  certificate with `SSL_CERT_FILE`. That works on Linux, not on the macOS host.

  Both runs gave the same result: 24 pass, 2 fail, 1 skip.

  - **`TestClient_core`:** all 3 pass.
  - **`TestClient_conformance`:** 17 of 18 pass, including the doubled slash,
  case, longest key, racing creates, racing swaps and the counter. It fails
  `should_refuse_a_create_and_a_swap_whose_content_does_not_match_its_checksum_w
  ith_ErrChecksum`:
    `create with a wrong checksum: got … outcome unknown: … StatusCode: 403 …
  api error SignatureDoesNotMatch …, want ErrChecksum` (the swap fails the same
  way).
  - **`TestClient_realBucket`:**
    - These pass: stores the checksum, URL and path characters (`a//b`,
  `trail/`, NFC and NFD, tab, newline and the rest), and 1024-byte keys (ASCII
  and `æ`).
    - It fails
  `should_refuse_a_wrong_checksum_of_empty_content_and_of_a_mebibyte…` with the
  same 403 on empty content.
    - It skips `should_fail_a_read…stored checksum does not match`: `the store
  refused a checksum header … 400 … BadDigest`.

  What the failures mean: the property is there, and only the error code
  differs. With curl I checked that the mismatched write stores nothing (a GET
  afterwards gives 404). `s3store` reports the 403 as "outcome unknown", which
  is safe, but the test fails. The skip is the test's own documented branch for
  a store that checks the header.

  **Extra probes with curl:**
  - A second `If-None-Match: *` gives `412 PreconditionFailed` with
  `<Condition>If-None-Match</Condition>`.
  - A stale `If-Match` gives 412.
  - `If-Match` on an absent key gives `404 NoSuchKey`.
  - After a delete, a listing shows 0 keys.
  - I could not provoke a 409.

  **Cleanup:** I removed the container and the image I built. The test container
  ran with `--rm`. I created no volumes, only bind mounts in the scratchpad.
  The repository is unchanged (`git status` is clean), and I did not touch
  `.env`.

  ## Comparison

  | Property | AWS emulator | versitygw v1.11.0 | Tigris |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Strong consistency (read, list) | Yes, M (in-memory, one lock, R) | Yes for
  core and conformance (M); a known race between ETag and body (R) | Yes (R) |
  | CAS `If-Match`: 412 stale, 404 absent | Yes, M | Yes, M | Yes, R |
  | Create-if-absent, one winner | Yes, M | Yes, M | Yes, 412 only, no 409 (R) |
  | Payload hash checked | Yes, but 403 `SignatureDoesNotMatch` (M) | Yes, M |
  Yes, 400 `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch` (R) |
  | `x-amz-checksum-sha256` | Checked, `BadDigest` (M) | Checked, M | Stored
  unchecked (R) |
  | `a//b` against `a/b` | Kept apart, M | Merged, M | Kept apart, R |
  | Trailing slash, `x` with `x/y`, 1024-byte keys | Pass, M | Fail, M | Pass, R
  |
  | Non-ASCII keys | Pass, M | Pass, M | Pass, R |
  | Path-style | Only path-style, M | Yes | Yes |
  | TLS | HTTPS only, self-signed, M | HTTP only | HTTPS, public CA |
  | Persistence | Memory only | Files | Durable |

  ## The differences that matter

  1. **The payload hash error code** is the only real gap. Two subtests fail in
  CI unless the test or `s3store` accepts this 403 for the emulator. Mapping a
  403 to `ErrChecksum` in `s3store` would be wrong for real stores.
  2. **The certificate is self-signed and new on each start.** CI must point
  `SSL_CERT_FILE` at `ca.pem` (Linux only), because `s3store` refuses
  `AWS_CA_BUNDLE`.
  3. **A CI run cannot test the path where the checksum header is not checked.**
  The emulator checks the header, so the "read detects a stored mismatched
  checksum" test always skips.

  A CI run against the emulator would show the core, CAS, create-if-absent,
  consistency at small scale, and key identity. It would not show Tigris's
  multi-region consistency, its latency, real contention, 409s, or behaviour
  under network faults. Those come from a single in-process lock, which is not
  representative.

  ## What I could not find out

  - I could not get it to answer a 409 `ConditionalRequestConflict` or
  `EntityTooLarge` (5 GiB is beyond the 2 GiB container limit).
  - I did not measure concurrency at Tigris's scale (thousands of writers).
  - I did not measure behaviour when a delete races a write.
  - I do not know whether the 403 is a tracked emulator issue: I found none in
  its `issues/` folder.

  All the scratch files were deleted except the run logs,
  `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-maragubot-Developer-s4ql/f1343515-adca-4ca0-9e
  b0-f507768f8fc7/scratchpad/emu/run1.txt` and
  `/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-maragubot-Developer-s4ql/f1343515-adca-4ca0-9e
  b0-f507768f8fc7/scratchpad/emu/run2.txt`.

⏺ The comparison is in, with our real-bucket tests run against both stand-ins.
  My recommendation: keep Tigris for s4ql's CI, through your shared workflow.

  ┌───────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────┐
  │                       │      AWS emulator       │ versitygw │  Tigris  │
  ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┤
  │ Core on the real      │                         │           │          │
  │ client                │ passes                  │ passes    │ passes   │

  ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┤

  └───────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴───────────┴──────────┘

  The AWS emulator is the better stand-in of the two, and it exists as you said.
  But for us it has costs:

  - Two of our tests fail on it, over that one error code. Accepting a 403 as
    "wrong checksum" would be wrong for real stores, so the tests would need an
    exception for the emulator.
  - It has no prebuilt image, serves HTTPS only with a self-signed certificate
    that is new on each start, and gets a release about every 1.5 hours,
    maintained by AI agents. That is a moving target under our CI.
  - It keeps everything in memory behind one lock, so it shows nothing about a
    real store under contention.

  Why Tigris: the spec gives real S3 the job of checking that our simulated
  store behaves like the real one. Each stand-in differs from Tigris in ways our
  own tests catch, so a stand-in checks the stand-in. And Tigris in CI is
  cheap: one run of all real-bucket tests takes 40 to 81 seconds, measured.

  And it can run as regular tests, as you wanted. Your shared workflow has an
  input secret-env that exports named secrets into the test step. So the plan
  is: no separate job, the test job gets the five values, and the tests fail
  when they are missing (skipping only under -short).

  Agree with Tigris through secret-env?

✻ Cogitated for 26s · done 10:59 AM

❯ I still don't understand what the sanitizer job does.                         

⏺ It runs the same tests a second time, in a mode that watches the C code for
  memory errors.

  Why we need that. s4ql calls SQLite, which is C, through our own binding. Go
  protects its own memory: a read past the end of a slice panics. C doesn't. If
  our binding hands SQLite a wrong length, or uses a statement after it was
  freed, C reads or writes memory it doesn't own. Often nothing visible happens:
  the test passes, and the damage shows up later as wrong data or a crash
  somewhere else.

  What the sanitizer does. With go test -asan, the C code is compiled with extra
  checks around every memory access. The moment C touches memory outside an
  allocation, or memory that was freed, the test stops with a report that names
  the line. So a silent corruption becomes a failing test.

  What the job runs, then:

  1. The normal tests, built that way. They don't test anything new; they just
     fail loudly if any of them makes C misuse memory.
  2. The fuzz tests, built that way. Fuzzing feeds random inputs to the binding
     and the parser, to reach paths the hand-written tests don't. Each runs for
     30 seconds.

  Why it's slow. The checked code runs several times slower, SQLite is one very
  large C file that gets compiled again with the checks, and the fuzz tests ran
  one after another.

  What it is not. The race detector, which the normal test job uses, finds Go
  goroutines touching the same data. It sees nothing of C's memory. The two
  catch different faults.

  Has it earned its place? It hasn't found a memory fault in the binding so far,
  as far as the diaries record. What it did catch were tests that depended on
  timing, because it's slow. I'd keep it: a memory fault in the binding is the
  kind of bug that corrupts data without an error, and this is the only check we
  have for it.
✻ Cogitated for 13s · done 11:02 AM

❯ Ah, now I understand. It's because we have our own CGo code because of our    
  own SQLite driver, right?                                                     

⏺ Yes, exactly. We chose our own small cgo binding to SQLite (the spec's "Our
  own SQLite binding") instead of an existing driver, so the C boundary is our
  code and ours to check. The cross-check module compares the binding's
  behaviour against mattn/go-sqlite3; the sanitizer checks that it doesn't
  misuse C's memory while doing so.

  1. Important: on `main` a newer pending run cancels the older pending one, so
  a code push followed quickly by a docs push would never have been tested. Each
  push now has its own concurrency group (`CI-<sha>`); pull requests keep
  theirs. Runs of close pushes now run side by side. Remaining gap: a run

⏺ Yes, and that was my doing. Every brief told the builder to run make test-asan
  before reporting: all tests of both modules under the sanitizer in a Linux
  container, plus every fuzz target. One such run took 7 min 19 s (measured,
  with 5 seconds per fuzz target), and builders often ran it more than once,
  after their review fixes. On top came the 50-fold runs of their own tests,
  with and without -race, at about 2.5 minutes each.

  The reason was sound when CI's sanitizer job took 24 minutes: a failure found
  there cost a long round trip. Now that job takes 6 minutes and runs in
  parallel with the rest, so the local run mostly duplicates CI.

  I changed the rule (0f4d4f2 in AGENTS.md): the sanitizer and the fuzz tests
  are CI's work. A builder runs them locally only when it changes the SQLite
  binding or a fuzz test, and then only for the packages it touched. What stays
  local: make test, make lint, and the repeated runs of new tests under -race,
  since those catch timing-dependent tests cheaply.

  The builders already running were briefed the old way; the next ones get the
  new rule.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  **Branch `reader-per-conn`, last commit `af46fda`** (11 commits on `9c5ba9b`,
  not pushed). Worktree:
  `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a199dcf1a4d74eb2f`.
  Diary: `docs/diary/2026-11-19-reader-per-conn.md`, steps 1 to 5.

  **Design**
  - Core: `DB.Reader(ctx)` opens a `*Reader` (one read connection, kept in
  `DB.readers`); `Reader.Read` is the old `DB.Read`; `Reader.Close` gives it
  back. `DB.Read`, the pool, and `Options.Readers` are gone.
  - Engine: `Engine.Conn()` gives a `*Conn` with `Exec`, `Query`, `Close`. It
  opens its reader at its first read. A `Conn`, because only the engine knows
  after parsing whether a statement reads.
  - Driver: a `driver.Conn` owns one `engine.Conn`. `go doc -all .` prints the
  same names.
  - Simulations: each caller owns a reader (core) or a `Conn` (engine). The
  simulator opens one for each read, and its request lost `readers`.

  **Decisions you should check**
  1. **A second query on one `sql.Conn` whose rows are open is now refused**
  with `ErrNotSupported`; before, it ran. Waiting is impossible: `database/sql`
  holds the connection's mutex during the driver call, so nothing could close
  the first rows. The old test "several queries at once on one connection" is
  replaced.
  2. **A failed open of a reader stops the instance** when `classify` calls it
  local (`CANTOPEN` included), through `DB.cacheError`.
  3. **The pool is unbounded by default**, so running out of file descriptors
  now stops the instance for good. A reviewer showed it with a limit of 64. Both
  reviewers suggest a default `SetMaxOpenConns` in `Open`. Not changed: it is
  yours and Markus's.
  4. **Simulation minimums changed in one place.** The workload whose callers
  never pause failed "reads refused by a clock" (0, minimum 5) after one new
  yield. Over 200 seeds, `main` reaches 88 and the branch 80, so the old 10
  seeds were luck. It now runs 30 seeds with minimums from 2026-11-19; that one
  minimum is 4 over 30 seeds, where it was 5 over 10.

  **Read checks on the new path**
  - Lease and clocks: `TestLease_clocks` and every `readErr` now go through
  `Reader.Read`.
  - Second check after the state is fixed, context, busy reader:
  `TestReader_Read`.
  - Spare refused, then caught up: `TestReader_Read`.
  - Stop and close: `TestReader_Close`, `TestDB_Reader`, including `DB.Close`
  waiting for a reader that is opening.
  - 22 hand-made mutants of core and driver: 19 caught, 2 fixed by removing
  code, 1 not catchable (`query_only`, as before).
  - Engine mutation tool on the new code: all 34 compiled mutants killed.

  **Checks on the final code, all passing**
  - New and changed tests 50 times with `-shuffle on`, and 50 times with
  `-race`.
  - `make test`, `make lint`, `make test-sql`.
  - `TestFree` under `-race`; simulator and cross-check modules under `-race`;
  `make check-browser`.
  - Core simulation: 0 differing traces, all 20 planted bugs found, worst within
  45 seeds. Engine: all 5 found.

  **Left to CI**
  - `make test-asan`: passed once before the review; I stopped the second run on
  your message. `faultvfs.VFS.OpenFiles` (the one change under
  `internal/sqlite`) was in the passing run. The review's change to `DB.Reader`
  has not run under the sanitizer.
  - Browser tests and `make site-check` (need Chromium).
  - `s3store` integration tests (real S3).

  **Not done**
  - A reader opens with `SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE`; if someone deletes the cache under
  a running instance, new connections fail with "no such table" and no stop. It
  needs a binding change.
  - `SetConnMaxIdleTime` and `SetConnMaxLifetime` have no test of their own (it
  would depend on time).
  - The open questions above are not in `docs/todo.md`.

  **Surprises**
  - Open readers are counted through `faultvfs` (two files each), because a root
  test cannot see the core.
  - A query on a new connection took 434 µs against 212 µs on a kept one;
  `database/sql` keeps two idle by default.

     requests run with -short. Cleanest, but it's a change in
     maragudk/workflows.
