✻ Crunched for 12s · done 12:17 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Review of `reader-per-conn` at `af46fda`

  **Verdict: sound to merge, with named follow-ups.** I found no correctness bug
  in the read checks, the life cycle, or the simulation. The findings are about
  file descriptors and documentation.

  ### Findings, by how much they matter

  **1. Running out of file descriptors stops the instance for good (decision
  4b). Matters.**
  `/internal/core/read.go:77` sends a failed open through `cacheError`. I
  reproduced it with a scratch test: with `RLIMIT_NOFILE` at 64, the 29th open
  query fails with `s4ql: the instance has stopped: local I/O failure: open a
  reader: sqlite: unable to open database file (code 14)`. An insert and a ping
  still fail after every row is closed and the limit is restored. Fix: a default
  bound on the pool in `Open` (see 4b).

  **2. Descriptors of closed readers are not given back until the instance
  closes. New, matters modestly, and makes 1 worse.**
  `lsof` on the test process, default VFS:

  | Moment | `cache.db` | `cache.db-wal` |
  |---|---|---|
  | Before | 1 | 1 |
  | 50 queries open | 51 | 51 |
  | Pool has closed every connection (`OpenConnections` 0) | 51 | 1 |
  | After `db.Close()` | 0 | 0 |

  SQLite's unix VFS keeps the descriptor of the main file while another
  connection of the process holds a lock on it, which the write connection
  always does, and reuses it for the next reader. So descriptors stay at the
  high-water mark of concurrent readers. `faultvfs.OpenFiles` reported 2 at the
  same moment: it counts SQLite's handles, not descriptors, so no root test can
  see this. The claims at `/s4ql.go:66` ("closes it when database/sql closes the
  connection") and `/docs/todo.md:67` are only half true. Fix: say so in both,
  and bound the pool.

  **3. `Reader.Close` closes the SQLite connection under `DB.mu`
  (`/internal/core/read.go:194-201`). True on paper.**
  The open was moved out of the mutex so that a slow disk holds nothing up; the
  close was not. With the default of two idle connections, every query beyond
  two at once closes a reader under the instance-wide mutex. Not measured. Fix:
  count closing readers as `DB.opening` counts opening ones.

  **4. `/internal/core/README.md:219` contradicts itself. Trivial.**
  It says the change "left the minimums of `TestSimulation` ... passing as they
  were", and a later paragraph says the minimums of one workload were
  re-derived.

  ### The three decisions

  **4a. Refusing a second read on a pinned connection: sound.**
  - `database/sql` holds the connection's mutex across every driver call, so
  waiting cannot work.
  - `database/sql` never runs a statement of its own on a connection with open
  rows; `sql.Conn.Close` waits for the rows, and transactions are not supported.
  - `ErrNotSupported` is acceptable but imprecise: everywhere else the text
  alone decides it, and here the state of the connection does.
  - Measured: a query on a `sql.Conn` right after cancelling the context of the
  one before it, without `Rows.Close`, was refused 1928 of 2000 times. The
  package documentation says this.
  - Revisit when transactions arrive: `sql.Tx` pins a connection, and a query
  inside a loop over rows is common SQLite usage.

  **4b. The builder is right by the letter, but I would bound the pool.**
  - Under exhaustion the write-ahead log's open at a first read and a write's
  spool file stop the instance too, so the choice is consistent.
  - A failed open of one more handle is neither a failed read nor a failed write
  of the cache, and failing a read's statement is always safe.
  - It can be told apart from a missing or damaged cache: `sqlite3_system_errno`
  gives `EMFILE`/`ENFILE` against `ENOENT`/`EACCES`. The binding does not
  expose it, and I did not run this.
  - A default `SetMaxOpenConns` is the fix that matters: it also bounds finding
  2 and protects the spool files. The number and any spec wording are Markus's.

  **4c. Matters little; operator error, and it fails closed.**
  - Reproduced: after deleting the cache files, every new connection's query
  fails with `s4ql: sqlite: no such table: main.s4ql_cache (code 1)`, while
  insert and ping succeed, and an empty `cache.db` is left behind.
  - Smallest right fix: open readers without `SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE`
  (`/internal/sqlite/sqlite.go:222`), so a missing file is `CANTOPEN` and stops
  the instance.
  - Without touching the binding: map a missing `s4ql_cache` in `conn.position`
  to `ErrCacheVersion`, which `cacheError` already stops on.
  - Related, on paper only: with the cache replaced by an older copy of itself,
  a new connection returned `[1]` after the insert of 2 was acknowledged, with
  no error. That is sabotage; I would not fix it.

  ### Checked and found sound

  - **The checks before a read.** Old `DB.Read` against new `Reader.Read`, line
  by line: both `servingLocked` checks sit at the same points relative to
  `begin` and `position()`. Only the wait for a pool permit is gone. `use(c)`
  guards every C call, and `rd.tx` is cleared only by its own transaction, after
  its rollback. A reader opened before a catch-up reads the new head (the
  builder's test, and the simulation's long-lived readers).
  - **Life cycle.** My stress tests passed under `-race`: 12 goroutines opening,
  reading, scanning, and closing readers against `DB.Close` and against a stop
  (60 rounds, twice), and 16 goroutines through `database/sql` against
  `db.Close` (30 rounds). No file was left open. `database/sql`'s mutex keeps
  `conn.Close`, the rows, and the lazy open of the reader apart.
  - **The simulation claim is true.** Reads refused by a clock over 300 seeds:

  | Workload | Base `9c5ba9b` | Head |
  |---|---|---|
  | One instance | 27 | 22 |
  | Everything | 166 | 151 |
  | Callers never pause | 129 | 169 |
  | Writes land late | 1634 | 1900 |

    The base rate for the changed workload is 4.3 per 10 seeds, below the old
  minimum of 5, so the old ten seeds were lucky. On both sides no pair of traces
  differed and no seed violated an invariant.
  - **Planted bugs.** Every one is found by its own symptom and every control is
  clean, in the default run of the core. The engine's simulation, with
  `keepRead`, passes too.
  - **Assertions.** None is weaker apart from that one minimum. The removed
  tests are the ones for the pool's wait.
  - **Tests.** The root tests run the real core; I found no timing dependence.

  ### Test runs

  - Root: `-race -shuffle on -count=5`.
  - Core: `-race -shuffle on -count=4` without the simulations; `TestSimulation`
  and `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` once without `-race`; `S4QL_SIM_SEEDS=300`
  on head and on base.
  - Engine: the whole package once; `TestConn` and `TestEngine_concurrent` under
  `-race -count=3`.
  - Simulator with `-tags s4ql_browser`, and `faultvfs`.

  All passed. The scratch tests are deleted and the worktree is clean at
  `af46fda`.

  ### Follow-ups

  1. Decide 4b: a default bound on the pool.
  2. Correct the descriptor claims in `/s4ql.go` and `/docs/todo.md`.
  3. Open readers without `CREATE`.
  4. Fix the README contradiction.
  5. Optional: close a reader's connection outside `DB.mu`.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ 2 background agents launched (↓ to manage)
   ├ Reader review follow-ups
   └ Convert skipped tests

⏺ #48 is merged (8294863): each pool connection now has a reader of its own. The
  independent review judged it sound: no fault in the checks before a read, the
  life cycle, or the simulation, and the lowered simulation minimum was right
  (measured over 300 seeds, the old ten seeds were lucky).

  Three small builders are running in parallel now:

  - Follow-ups of that review: a reader must not create the cache, and two
    corrections to what the documentation claims.
  - The other ten skipped tests, with a lint rule so the "never skip" rule keeps
    itself.
  - The real-bucket tests as regular tests (started earlier).

  One decision for you, from the review. With the pool unbounded by default, a
  burst of queries can use up the process's file descriptors, and that stops the
  instance for good. The reviewer reproduced it: with a limit of 64
  descriptors, the 29th open query failed, and the instance stayed stopped after
  the limit was restored. It also found that SQLite keeps a reader's descriptor
  of the cache file until the instance closes, so descriptors stay at the peak.

  I recommend that Open sets a default limit on the pool, derived from the
  process's descriptor limit and not a number picked by hand: readers may use at
  most half of it. An application can still change it with SetMaxOpenConns, as
  with any *sql.DB. Agree?

✻ Crunched for 1m 25s · done 12:20 PM

❯ sounds reasonable                                                             

⏺ fabrik:builder(Default limit on the pool)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ A builder is on the default pool limit: the process's file-descriptor limit
  divided by four (a reader costs up to two descriptors, and the readers get
  half), set by Open and changeable with SetMaxOpenConns.

  One limit of the design you should know: with Register and sql.Open by name,
  database/sql creates the *sql.DB and the driver never sees it, so the default
  can't be applied there. The documentation of Register will say that the
  application sets SetMaxOpenConns itself.

  Four small builders are running in parallel now: this one, the review
  follow-ups, the skipped tests, and the real-bucket tests. Nothing is waiting
  on you.

✻ Crunched for 23s · done 12:26 PM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The default bound is built on branch `pool-limit`, last commit `5d3f650`. All
  checks the brief asked for pass.

  **Function.** `maxOpenConns(limit uint64, limited bool) int` in `s4ql.go`
  gives `limit/4`, at least 1 and at most `math.MaxInt`, and 0 when there is no
  limit.
  - On this Mac it gives 15360. I measured it: `syscall.Getrlimit` returns a
  soft limit of 61440, because Go raises the soft limit at start (capped at
  `kern.maxfilesperproc` on Darwin). The shell says 1048576.
  - I could not measure a GitHub runner. In `golang:1.27` containers, Docker's
  default limit of 1048576 gives 262144. A container with soft 1024 and hard
  65536 gave 65535 (Go raised it), so 16383. The tests compare against the live
  value, not a fixed number.

  **Platforms.** `filelimit_unix.go` (`//go:build unix`) reads the limit with
  `Getrlimit`. `filelimit_other.go` (`!unix`, so Windows, js, wasip1, plan9)
  gives no limit. I vetted copies of both files on 27 platforms; the root
  package itself does not build without cgo.

  **Not expected:** `RLIM_INFINITY` is -1 on Linux, -3 on Solaris and `1<<64-1`
  on loong64. Only `uint64(RLIM_INFINITY & math.MaxUint64)` compiles everywhere;
  my first attempt failed on loong64.

  **`Register`.** It sets no bound and logs `pool.max_open_conns=0`. Its doc
  says the application calls `SetMaxOpenConns` itself, and why. `Open` logs the
  bound it sets.

  **Tests.** A table test, `TestFileLimit` against `Getrlimit` (it also accepts
  an unlimited limit), the bound after `Open`, `SetMaxOpenConns` overriding it,
  and `Register` with no bound. `TestRows` already covers queries waiting for a
  connection. The fd-exhaustion scenario is left out.

  **Checks.** The changed tests ran `-count=50 -shuffle on`, and also under
  `-race` with `make test` running at the same time; `make test`, `make lint`
  and `make check-browser` pass.

  **Decision for you.** The bound is per instance, so two instances in one
  process can use every descriptor. I documented it in `Open` and `docs/todo.md`
  rather than add a shared budget. `docs/todo.md` also lists the review
  findings I left.

  The diary is `docs/diary/2026-11-19-pool-limit.md`.

  Created PR #52 (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Default limit on the pool" finished · 13m 53s

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The default pool limit is built, in pull request #52; CI is running it.

  What it does in practice, measured, and it's less than the idea suggests: the
  bound comes out very high. Go raises the process's soft descriptor limit at
  start, so on this Mac the limit is 61,440 and the bound 15,360; in a default
  Docker container the bound is 262,144.

  So it does what we agreed, the readers can never use more than half of the
  descriptors, and the review's failure can't happen by that route any more. But
  it is not a throttle: a burst can still open thousands of SQLite connections,
  which costs memory. An application that wants fewer sets SetMaxOpenConns, as
  with any driver, and the documentation says so.

  One more limit of it, documented: the bound is per instance, so two s4ql
  databases in one process could together use every descriptor. I left that as a
  note and didn't build a shared budget.

  Still running: CI on #50 and #52, the review follow-ups, and the skipped
  tests.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Branch `reader-follow-ups` is done; last commit `d86567b`. Four of the five
  points are done. I tried point 3, took it back, and recorded it in
  `docs/todo.md`.

  1. **Readers no longer create the cache.** Both bindings have a new
  `sqlite.OpenExistingVFS(path, vfs)`, which opens without the create flag (the
  browser's VFS already refuses a missing file without it). Only the write
  connection in `Open` can create the cache. New tests in the binding (native
  and browser), in `TestDB_Reader` in the core, and in `TestStop` at the root
  delete the files under a serving instance. The next reader stops the instance
  with `...open a reader: sqlite: unable to open database file (code 14)`, no
  file is left after the close, and a new start rebuilds from the store. With
  the old open, the root test fails with the reviewer's `no such table:
  main.s4ql_cache`.
  2. **File descriptors.** My `lsof` run matched the reviewer's: 51/51, then
  51/1, then 0/0. Twenty new queries after that stayed at 51 descriptors of the
  cache, so SQLite reuses them. Corrected in `s4ql.go`, `docs/todo.md` and the
  core README; numbers are in the diary.
  3. **Not done.** Closing outside the mutex passed the life-cycle tests, the
  simulation and the new wait test. But the sanitizer then failed
  `TestClose/should_refuse_a_query_that_comes_while...` with a `-wal` left
  behind. Natively the log was left in 21 of 300 runs, 3 of 100 under `-race`,
  and 0 of 300 without the change. When two connections close at once, SQLite's
  last close cannot lock the file alone, so it leaves the log. A fix needs a
  separate lock for closes, which is no longer simple. Recorded as known in
  `docs/todo.md`; the comment in `Reader.Close` now says why.
  4. **README minimums.** Corrected, with the 300-seed measurement.
  5. **Todo.** (a) and (b) are recorded under the driver. Nothing built.

  The code-review skill found three wording issues and nothing blocking; all
  three are fixed.

  `-race`. `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser` passed. The
  sanitizer passed in Docker on `internal/sqlite`, `internal/core` and the root
  package. The new browser tests passed in headless Chromium.

  As of 2026-11-19:

  - **Built: slice 1, end to end.** A database is a log of commits in S3, with a
  local SQLite cache. One instance at a time holds a lease on it. Commits,
  recovery after a crash, and replay of the log work. They are tested in a
  deterministic simulation, and in tests against a real S3-compatible bucket,
  which run in CI on every pull request. The engine runs `create table`,
  `insert`, `select *`, and `drop table` for one integer column. It is checked
  against SQLite statement by statement, in a simulation with faults, and by
  fuzzing. The SQL parser agrees with SQLite on all 138,926 statements on which
  SQLite gives a clear verdict, of a corpus of 139,234. The package
  `maragu.dev/s4ql` is a `database/sql` driver, so an application can run these
  statements on a bucket. It is checked against SQLite through `database/sql`,
  in 200 generated sequences of 40 statements and by fuzzing. A cache belongs to
  one database, and another database refuses it.

  What I chose for the slice, say if you want it different:

  - Form: a plain ? only, where the insert of slice 1 has its integer: insert 
    into t1 (v1) values (?). ?1, :name, @name, $name and sql.Named are rejected
    as not supported.
  - Values: Go integers only. nil, strings, floats, bools, bytes and times are
    rejected as not supported.
  - Count: the number of arguments must equal the number of ?, otherwise an
    error and nothing runs. SQLite's C API would bind NULL for a missing one.
  - Log: the commit object keeps the arguments next to the statement's text. The
    format already has the field, so it does not change.

  simulation to use arguments.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Slice 2 (parameters) is built on branch `slice-2-parameters`, which starts at
  `origin/main` 10ed900 and has 10 commits. `make test` and `make lint` pass,
  and so do `go build -tags s4ql_browser ./...` and `make check-browser`. The
  differential test in `internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck` still agrees
  with its oracle, and its pinned excused counts (816 and 14) did not change. I
  did not push and did not open a pull request.

  The worktree has no S3 credentials, so I ran the root package without `-short`
  with `-skip TestOpen_realBucket`; CI's job with credentials runs that test.

  ## What I built

  - **The statement pass (`internal/sql/stmt`).** `Insert` has a new field
  `Parameter int`: 1 for a plain `?`, 0 for a literal. Every statement now has
  `Parameters() int`. The recognizer accepts a plain `?` as the value of an
  insert and rejects:
    - `?NNN` as `numbered parameter`
    - `:a`, `@a`, `$a` and `#a` as `named parameter`
    - `-?` as `parameter after a sign`

    The tests read SQLite's count of parameters as the largest index that
  `sqlite3_bind_int64` accepts before it returns SQLITE_RANGE, because the
  binding has no `bind_parameter_count`. `internal/sqlite` is unchanged, so no
  sanitizer run was needed. For every supported text, `checkMeaning` compares
  the count with SQLite's, and an insert of a parameter runs in SQLite bound to
  a value at or near the 64-bit limits. There are 2 supported cases and 27 near
  misses in `check_test.go`, including `?0`, `?32767`, `#1`, `(? ?)`, `-?` and
  `cast(? as int)`. `default ?` turned out to be a syntax error in SQLite, and
  the test now expects that. The near-miss templates and the README's results
  table are updated.
  - **The engine.** `Conn.Exec` and `Conn.Query` take `args ...*s4qlpb.Value`.
  `prepare` checks the text, then the number of arguments, then that each one is
  an integer, all before any transaction. The plan has a new
  `*Parameter{Number}` node, which `Format` prints as `?1`. The arguments go
  into `core.Tx.Annotate`.
    - New exports: the sentinel `ErrArguments`, and `engine.Check(src, n)`,
  which gives the text and count errors without the database and without logging
  an event.
    - The statement event has a new outcome, `wrong_arguments`, at Debug.
    - Tests:
      - cross-checks against SQLite through `pin`, which now also compares the
  state after a write, including the duplicate key against a literal and against
  the stored names;
      - the wrong count for every kind of statement;
      - non-integer values;
      - the order of the checks, with a context that has ended and on a closed
  instance;
      - replay from an empty cache;
      - reading the annotations from the commit objects;
      - no argument value in any event.
  - **The driver (`conn.go`).**
    - `CheckNamedValue` applies `database/sql`'s default conversion. It never
  fails, and keeps a value it cannot convert to report after the text is
  checked.
    - `arguments` converts each argument. Only when one cannot be converted does
  it call `engine.Check` first, so that the text's error, then the count's,
  come before the argument's. The text is parsed once when every argument
  converts.
    - `NumInput` stays -1.
    - The legacy `Stmt.Exec` and `Stmt.Query` used to drop argument values; they
  now pass them through.
    - Tests in `TestParameters`, plus changes to `TestExec`, `TestPrepare`,
  `TestConn_Raw`, `TestStop` and `TestError`.
  - **drivercheck.** A text with no parameter given an argument now gets the
  driver's count error. That error is checked against `engine.Check` and not
  compared with mattn. An insert with one parameter and one argument runs and is
  compared with mattn. `TestGenerated` now requires the outcome kind `insert
  wrong number of arguments` instead of `insert not supported`, which only an
  argument could produce.
  - **Documents.** `docs/roadmap.md` has the section "Slice 2: parameters".
  `docs/todo.md` has a section "Parameters" with every decision of detail. The
  READMEs of the engine and the pass, the `s4ql` package documentation, and the
  diary `docs/diary/2026-11-19-slice-2-parameters.md` (3 steps) are updated.

  ## Decisions of detail (all recorded in docs/todo.md under "Parameters")

  - **Wrong count:** `s4ql: wrong number of arguments: 2 for a statement with 1
  parameter`, with "parameters" for any number but 1. It matches
  `engine.ErrArguments` and none of the public sentinels. No public sentinel was
  added; please confirm.
  - **Order of the checks:** the text, then the count, then each argument in
  order, its name before its type. So `select * from t1` with `"x"` gives the
  count error.
  - **`NumInput` is -1.** Otherwise `database/sql` would refuse the call itself
  with `sql: expected 1 arguments, got 2`, before the driver sees it.
  - **Type errors:**
    - Another type: `s4ql: not supported: argument 1 of type string`. The type
  is the one after conversion (`float32` becomes `float64`). nil, an invalid
  `sql.NullInt64` and a nil pointer all give `of type nil`.
    - A named argument: `s4ql: not supported: the named argument "v"`.
    - A value the conversion refuses: `argument 1 of type T, which database/sql
  cannot convert`, with the conversion's error as the cause and not in the
  message. This includes a failing `driver.Valuer`. Whether an application's
  Valuer error should count as "not supported" at all is for you to decide.
  - **Overflow (found in self-review):** `database/sql`'s default converter
  turns `uint(1<<63)`, and any named type of kind `uint`, into a negative
  `int64`; it refuses only `uint64`. The driver now refuses every unsigned
  integer above `MaxInt64`, and a pointer to one, as `argument 1 of type uint,
  whose value does not fit a signed 64-bit integer`.
  - **`?0` and `?32767`** are `numbered parameter`. SQLite's grammar-action
  error `variable number must be between ?1 and ?32766` is not reproduced, as
  with slice 1's other grammar-action errors; the stmt README says so.
  - **Non-integer value in the engine:** `s4ql: not supported: argument 1, which
  is not an integer`, which matches `stmt.ErrNotSupported`. `publicError` maps
  any error wrapping that sentinel to `ErrNotSupported`.
  - **No event for driver-level argument refusals.** A named argument or one
  that cannot be converted is refused through `engine.Check`, which logs
  nothing. The same statement with an integer argument and the wrong count does
  log an event.

  ## Where this differs from the brief

  - The order "count before type" and the overflow rejection are my additions
  inside the agreed slice.
  - I added the outcome `wrong_arguments` to telemetry although the brief said
  no new telemetry is needed. Without it the count error was logged as
  `core_error` at Warn.
  - `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm go build -tags s4ql_browser ./...` fails in
  `internal/sql/oracle` (`DenyProcessPragmas undefined`). This was already the
  case before my change, and that package is not built for the browser. `make
  check-browser` and the native build with the tag both pass.

  ## Self-review

  The code-review skill (high) found no important bugs and six nits. I fixed
  four:
  - A Valuer's error was formatted into the message and not kept as the cause.
  - Three comments were not re-wrapped.
  - A prepared QueryRow was missing from the wrong-count test.
  - Half of one engine test bound no argument.

  I left two and noted them in todo.md: no public sentinel for the count error,
  and no event for driver-level argument refusals. Fixing the first nit led to
  the uint overflow fix.

✻ Sautéed for 39s · done 2:07 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  I changed no files. Paths are relative to /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql.

  **1. docs/spec.md**
  - Isolation and the single writer (l.32–33): "a second writer waits until the
  first has committed or rolled back", and "Each read transaction sees one
  consistent state of the cache, even while a write is in progress." The spec
  names no isolation level.
  - ACID (l.38): "Transactions are ACID within one database."
  - Lease (l.44): "Reads and new write transactions fail at once with a
  temporary error. A write transaction that is already open commits only if its
  manifest swap still succeeds."
  - What a commit is (l.56): "The transaction is committed when the swap
  succeeds, and not before."
  - Size (l.57): "its commit fails at once with an error that says so. The limit
  is 5 GiB, or less".
  - Acknowledgement and outcome unknown (l.60–61): "A read that starts after a
  commit is acknowledged therefore always sees it." "Commit returns an 'outcome
  unknown' error only if the caller gives up first… The instance then serves
  nothing until it knows."
  - Stop (l.62): "Running reads and waiting writes fail".
  - Annotations (l.51): "a commit object stores the SQL statements that produced
  them… replay never depends on them."
  - l.48 says "no snapshots in the first design". This is about S3 storage, not
  read isolation.

  **2. todo.md / roadmap.md**
  - roadmap l.34: "without parameters and without `db.BeginTx`". l.53: "Each
  statement is its own transaction." l.56: `begin` is rejected.
  - todo l.90, the one item marked "To look at again with transactions": "a
  second query on one pinned connection whose rows are still open is refused…
  With transactions, a `sql.Tx` pins one too, and a query inside the loop over
  the rows of another query is common use of SQLite, which this refuses."
  - todo l.54, on "too large": "A slice that makes 'too large' reachable, with
  transactions or larger values, should give it a name". Today it has no
  sentinel.
  - todo l.35: a transaction that changes nothing writes no commit object, so
  its annotations are dropped.
  - todo l.47: `BeginTx` gives `s4ql: not supported: transactions`. l.56: it
  gives the same error on a stopped instance.
  - Neither file has an open question about isolation or savepoints.

  **3. internal/core** (tx.go, read.go, core.go)
  - `DB.Begin(ctx)` waits for `writerLock` through `acquireServing`, runs `begin
  immediate` on the one write connection, and records `parent`.
  - `writerLock` "is held for a whole write transaction, and by the lease task
  while it takes the lease and catches up" (core.go:175).
  - Methods: `CreateTable`, `DropTable`, `Upsert`, `Delete`, `Get`, `Scan`,
  `Table`, `Tables`, `Annotate`, `Commit`, `Rollback`.
  - It reads its own writes: "so it reads its own writes, and nothing else sees
  them until Tx.Commit".
  - Any number of calls and statements fit in one Tx. Touched keys go in temp
  tables, and annotations go in a spool "in the order in which they were made".
  - After an error of the cache, "the transaction can only be rolled back".
  - `Scan`: "must not change the table while it scans it, and must not drop any
  table".
  - Every call checks `usable()`: stopped, not the holder, or not serving. It
  does not check the clocks.
  - `publish` refuses if `d.man.Head != tx.parent`.
  - core README l.63: "A write transaction has no maximum duration, for now."
  - l.178: a stop closes an idle transaction's connection, and "Tx.Rollback…
  then return nil".
  - `Reader.Read` starts a `ReadTx` fixed at one position. The lease is checked
  against both clocks at the start only, and later calls check only for a stop.
  It fails with `ErrReaderBusy` and does not wait.
  - Core assumes nothing about one statement per transaction. Its simulation
  already runs several operations per Tx.

  **4. internal/engine** (engine.go)
  - `Conn.write` does `c.begin`, then `buildSink`, then `run`, then
  `tx.annotate(sql)`, then `tx.commit`. A deferred `rollback` runs on any
  failure, so every write call begins and ends its own Tx.
  - `Conn.query` opens a `ReadTx` on the connection's reader, and the `Rows`
  hold it until they end.
  - `Conn` holds only `rd`. The README says a Conn "is also where the state of a
  connection will belong, such as a transaction that spans statements."
  - For a transaction across calls, `Conn` would have to keep the open
  `writeTx`. `write` would then skip begin and commit. `query` would read from
  the `writeTx`, which already implements `reader`, instead of `c.read`. The
  engine would also need commit and rollback entry points.
  - internal/engine/simulation_test.go:764 asserts that each commit holds
  exactly one statement: `"commit %v holds %v statements"`.

  **5. Driver**
  - conn.go:302–309: `BeginTx` and `Begin` return
  `notSupported("transactions")`. The comment says "each statement is a
  transaction of its own."
  - errors.go:112 maps `core.ErrReaderBusy` to "s4ql: not supported: a statement
  that reads on a connection whose rows of an earlier query are open".
  - Why, from core README l.36: "database/sql holds the mutex of a connection
  during every call of the driver… A second query… that waited inside the driver
  for the rows of the first would hold that mutex, so nothing could close or
  read those rows."

  **6. internal/sql/stmt**
  - There is no transaction support. The recognizer rejects any command it does
  not know.
  - check_test.go:446: `begin` gives `"s4ql: not supported: BEGIN (at offset
  0)"`.
  - vocabulary.go:183–187 has the names BEGIN, COMMIT, ROLLBACK, SAVEPOINT and
  RELEASE for these messages.

  **7. Tests**
  - **internal/sim** is only the scheduler harness. The workloads are elsewhere.
    - The engine simulation (internal/engine/simulation_test.go) runs callers,
  each with an `engine.Conn`, issuing single statements from
  `stmttest.Sequence`. Each statement carries an `/* id N */` comment and is
  tracked as an `attempt`.
    - The check: each commit holds one statement, and each statement's result
  matches SQLite on the state after the commit it saw.
    - The core simulation already runs 1–5 operations per Tx, with holds,
  1-in-10 rollbacks and a map model (`mtx`).
  - **drivercheck** runs each text through one `call` (exec, query,
  prepare+exec, prepare+query, query row, exec with an argument, begin) on s4ql
  and on mattn's driver. It does this pinned on a `sql.Conn` and through the
  pool, and compares outcomes and final tables. `callBegin` only expects BeginTx
  to fail.
  - **stmttest** generates single statements of slice 1. `Sequence.Next()`
  returns one `(src, query, supported)` at a time, with no notion of a
  transaction.

  **Open design questions**
  - **Isolation level offered:** not decided. The spec has only the single
  writer and "one consistent state" per read transaction.
  - **Whether a read-only Tx is a snapshot:** partly decided. "Each read
  transaction sees one consistent state of the cache" (l.33). Whether
  `BeginTx{ReadOnly}` maps to a read transaction is not decided.
  - **Several open writers queue or fail:** decided. "a second writer waits
  until the first has committed or rolled back" (l.32). A timeout for that wait
  is not decided.
  - **Read-your-own-writes:** not in the spec. Core gives it.
  - **Reads inside a write Tx during open rows**, the pinned-connection refusal:
  open (todo l.90).
  - **A statement that fails inside a Tx** (whether the Tx stays usable, as
  SQLite does for most errors): not decided. Core poisons the Tx only on cache
  errors.
  - **DDL inside a Tx:** not decided. Core supports it, but a scan blocks a
  drop.
  - **The SQL texts `begin`/`commit`/`rollback`/`savepoint`:** not decided
  beyond the roadmap's "rejected".
  - **A lost lease during an open Tx:** decided. "A write transaction that is
  already open commits only if its manifest swap still succeeds" (l.44). Calls
  in core fail before the commit.
  - **Instance stop:** decided. "Running reads and waiting writes fail" (l.62).
  - **A maximum duration of a Tx:** not decided (core README: "no maximum
  duration, for now").
  - **A public "too large" error:** open (todo l.54). The spec requires only "an
  error that says so".
  - **Isolation levels or options other than the default in `TxOptions`:** not
  decided.
  - **Annotations of a Tx that changes nothing are dropped:** decided in todo
  l.35, not in the spec.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Survey transactions groundwork" finished · 1m 47s

⏺ The research on transactions is back, and it leaves one design question that
  is yours. Here is the first, and the only one I need before the slice can be
  defined.

  When does a transaction take the single writer's place?

  The spec already says there is one writer and a second one waits. It does not
  say when db.BeginTx becomes that writer.

  1. At BeginTx (my recommendation). Like SQLite's begin immediate. Every
     transaction sees the newest state and its own writes, and its commit can
     never fail because someone else wrote in between. The cost is that an open
     transaction blocks all other writes until it ends, also while it only
     reads. Reads outside transactions are not blocked.
  2. At its first write. Like SQLite's plain begin. Transactions that only read
     do not block writers. The cost is a new kind of failure: a transaction that
     read, and then writes after someone else committed, fails and must be
     retried by the application.

  With option 1, a transaction opened with sql.TxOptions{ReadOnly: true} would
  be the way to read a consistent state without blocking anyone. The core
  already has both pieces, so option 1 needs no new mechanism.

  Slice 2's checks are half done, 8 of 16 passed so far and none failed. Usage
  is at 79%.

  Still waiting on you: when a transaction takes the writer's place, at BeginTx
  (my recommendation) or at its first write. I won't start transactions before
  you answer.

  Transactions have started. A builder is on the engine part, with your
  decision: a transaction is the writer from BeginTx, and ReadOnly: true gives a
  read transaction on one consistent state that blocks no one. The driver part
  follows on its merged code. The builder for the generated checks of parameters
  is still running beside it.

  What I decided for the slice, say if you want something different:

  - A failed statement inside a transaction leaves it open and usable, as in
    SQLite.
  - create table and drop table work inside a transaction and a rollback undoes
    them.
  - A write in a read-only transaction fails with SQLite's own error for that.
  - begin, commit, rollback as SQL text stay rejected. Transactions go through
    db.BeginTx only.
  - A statement while rows of an earlier query are open in the same transaction
    stays refused for now. That rules out writing inside a loop over a query's
    rows, which is common. I'd take it as its own slice afterwards.

      123  - **Not built: a refusal of a write as too large that is not `
           400 EntityTooLarge`.** `internal/store/s3store` maps only that
            answer to `store.ErrTooLarge`. A store or a proxy in front of
            it that refuses a large write in another way, such as with HT
           TP 413, gives an unknown outcome, and the commit uploads the s
           ame bytes again until the caller gives up. Nothing is committe
           d wrongly, but the caller gets the wrong error, and each attem
           pt may upload gigabytes. Found on 2026-11-18 with the limits o
           f the stores: Amazon S3 takes 5 GiB in one write, Cloudflare R
           2 5 MiB less, Ceph what it is configured for, MinIO 5 TiB; Tig
           ris documents none.
      124  - **Not decided: catch-up downloads every missing commit objec
           t twice.** It reads each one on the walk back through the log,
            and again when it applies it. A spool of the walk could remov
           e the second download.
      125  - **Known: `Reader.Close` closes its SQLite connection while i
           t holds `DB.mu`,** so a close that stalls on a slow disk holds
            up every call of the instance and the lease task meanwhile. `
           DB.Reader` opens without the mutex since pull request #48, and
            the review of that pull request asked for the close to follow
           . A builder tried it on 2026-11-19: the close marked the conne
           ction closed and counted itself under the mutex, closed it wit
           hout the mutex, and `DB.Close` waited for the count, as it wai
           ts for the readers that are opening. It kept every life-cycle
           test and the simulation passing, and a new test showed the wai
           t. But it let the close of a reader run at the same time as th
           e closes of `DB.Close`, and SQLite then sometimes left the wri
           te-ahead log behind: the close of the last connection deletes
           it only if that close can lock the cache alone, and two closes
            at once can keep each other from that. `TestClose` "should re
           fuse a query that comes while the close closes the rows that a
           re open, before the instance has stopped" in the root package,
            whose `database/sql` closes idle connections while the connec
           tor closes the instance, found a log left in 21 of 300 runs, 3
            of 100 under `-race`, and once in a run under the address san
           itizer, against 0 of 300 before. A log that is left is no loss
           , because the next start recovers from it, but the tests take
           a log that is left for a connection that is left. A fix would
           close the connections one at a time under a lock of their own,
            which `DB.closeIdleLocked` would then wait for while it holds
            `DB.mu`. That is no longer simple, so the code was left as it
            was. The diary is `docs/diary/2026-11-19-reader-follow-ups.md
           `.
      126 +- **To consider: a spare that keeps its cache up to date.** To
          +day an instance without the lease catches up its cache only wh
          +en it gets the lease, so a spare that waited long has the whol
          +e log since its start to download and apply before it serves. 
          +Markus asked on 2026-11-19: "Should the cache keep up to date 
          +on a spare instance even when it doesn't have the lease (but s
          +till reject reads and writes)?" It would shorten the time unti
          +l a spare serves after it takes over, at the cost of reads fro
          +m S3 while it waits. Not decided.                             
      127  - **Not decided: a connection whose close fails.** `DB.Close`
           marks the instance as closed before it knows that the connecti
           ons closed, so a connection whose close fails is never closed.
            Since the fix of the scan, only an `iter.Pull` of a scan with
           out its `stop` can cause that.
      128  - **A possible later addition: S3's timestamps could shorten t
           he wait after a crash.** They would be switched on only for a
           store that has been checked. They help how fast an instance se
           rves, not safety. Why S3's clock is not safe enough to rest th
           e lease on is in [research/s3-clock.md](research/s3-clock.md).

⏺ It's in docs/todo.md under the storage core, as a question to consider later
  (c0cdfb2).

  Today a spare catches up only when it gets the lease, so one that waited long
  has the whole log since its start to download before it serves. Keeping up
  while it waits would shorten that takeover, at the cost of reads from S3 in
  the meantime.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  ## Report: transactions in the engine (branch `tx-engine`)

  Branch `tx-engine` in worktree
  /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a15a9829a503f3c3c. It
  starts at `origin/main` 429dd5c and has 6 commits (13b25cc..71ffff3). Nothing
  is pushed.

  All of points 1 to 11 are built and tested. `make test` passes, `make lint`
  passes, and `make check-browser` passes, so the build with the tag
  `s4ql_browser` works. I did not change `internal/simulator`. The new tests
  pass with `-count=20`, with and without `-race`.

  ### API added

  **Engine** (`internal/engine/tx.go`, which is new, plus `engine.go`):
  - `Conn.Begin(ctx) error` begins a write transaction. It waits for the
  writer's place until the context ends, and has no deadline of its own.
  - `Conn.BeginReadOnly(ctx) error` begins a read-only transaction. It is a read
  transaction on the reader of the connection and waits for nothing.
  - `Conn.Commit(ctx) error` and `Conn.Rollback(ctx) error`.
  - `Conn.Close` now rolls back an open transaction.
  - New errors:
    - `ErrTooLarge`: also matches `core.ErrTooLarge`. It applies to statements
  outside a transaction too. The message starts with `s4ql: the transaction is
  too large to commit: `.
    - `ErrRowsOpen`, `ErrTxOpen`, `ErrNoTx` and `ErrTxDone`.
  - `sqlerr.CodeReadOnly` (8).
  - A new event `transaction` with these attributes: `transaction.kind`, `.end`,
  `.statements`, `.writes`, `.position`, `commit.parent`, `outcome`, `error`,
  `duration.total`. Events of statements inside a transaction also carry
  `transaction.kind`.
  - Two new outcomes, both at Debug: `tx_state` and `too_large`. `ErrRowsOpen`
  is logged as `not_supported`.

  **Core:**
  - `Tx.Serving(ctx)` and `ReadTx.Serving(ctx)` check the lease against the
  clocks, as `Begin` and `Read` do.
  - `Tx.Savepoint`, `Tx.Release` and `Tx.RollbackTo`: one savepoint at a time. A
  rollback to the savepoint restores the cache and the transaction's in-memory
  record of each table (what its commit object is written from).
  - A commit while a savepoint is open is refused as not committed, with
  `ErrInvalid`.
  - A rollback to a savepoint taken before an annotation fails the transaction,
  because the spool of annotations only grows. The engine never does this,
  because it annotates last.

  ### Decisions of detail

  All of these are in docs/todo.md under "Transactions", marked for the lead to
  confirm.
  - **A savepoint around every write in a write transaction.** A failed
  statement, or a panic, rolls back to it. Without it, a lease lost between the
  upsert and the annotation, an ended context or a panic would leave the
  statement's row in a transaction that then commits. Queries take no savepoint.
  - **Order of checks for a statement in a transaction:** text and arguments
  first, then the context, then `Serving` (the clocks, as for a statement of its
  own), then whether rows are open.
  - **A write in a read-only transaction** first gets SQLite's errors about
  names that SQLite gives when it prepares the statement, looked up in the
  transaction's state. Then it fails with `attempt to write a readonly
  database`, code 8, extended code 8. I checked this against SQLite under
  `pragma query_only = 1`. A refused insert sets the count of changes to 0, as
  SQLite does; a refused create or drop leaves the count alone.
  - **Begin is refused with `ErrRowsOpen`** while rows of an earlier query on
  the connection are open, including rows of a statement outside a transaction.
  It gives `ErrTxOpen` while a transaction is open.
  - **Open rows inside a transaction:** every statement fails with
  `ErrRowsOpen`, including writes, which SQLite would run. The scan holds an
  SQLite statement on the transaction's connection.
  - **Commit or rollback while rows are open:**
    - Rows that no loop is reading are ended first, and a later `All` gives
  nothing.
    - Rows that a loop is reading cannot be ended from outside. The loop ends at
  its next row with `ErrTxDone`, and the real end of the transaction (the
  rollback, or the end of the read) runs when the loop ends. Until then the
  writer's place stays taken, statements on the connection give `ErrRowsOpen`,
  and `Close` gives `ErrReaderInUse`.
    - In that case a commit of a write transaction rolls back and fails with an
  error matching `ErrRowsOpen`. The reason: SQLite cannot drop the core's
  temporary tables while a statement on the connection reads, and an aborted
  statement finished later would race the next writer on the shared write
  connection.
    - In that case a commit of a read-only transaction just ends it and returns
  nil.
    - The transaction event is logged at the real end.
    - `database/sql` closes a transaction's rows before it calls the driver's
  Commit or Rollback, so a driver whose `Rows.Close` ends the loop will not hit
  this.
  - **Lease lost or instance stopped:** statements give the refusal a statement
  outside a transaction would get, and the core's commit decides the outcome
  through its manifest swap. Rollback frees the writer's place. It returns nil,
  except after a stop caused by a local failure, where the rollback in the cache
  can return its error. A read-only commit on a stopped instance returns nil.
  - **Commit or rollback with no open transaction** gives `ErrNoTx`. A failed
  begin logs no event. `transaction.statements` counts every statement given
  while the transaction was open, failed ones included.
  - **Planted bugs:** inside a transaction only `skipUnique` applies. `keepRead`
  also leaves rows of a transaction open.
  - **Lowering the size limit in tests:** `simstore.Options.MaxSize`. The tests
  use 4 KiB, which 100 inserts exceed.

  ### Differences from the brief

  - I changed one existing test file where my change forced it. `pair.step` in
  `differential_test.go` now accepts that a refused insert in a read-only
  transaction sets the count of changes to 0, which is what SQLite does. It
  identifies an insert with `stmt.Check`.
  - The fakes in `operator_internal_test.go` and `memory_internal_test.go` got
  no-op methods for the new storage calls.
  - `TestConn_differential` reuses `newPair`, `pair.step` and `newSequence`. If
  the other builder changes their signatures, the merge will need a small fix.

  ### Not built or not covered

  - The driver.
  - Generators of transactions in `stmttest` and `drivercheck`.
  - A simulation workload with transactions, planted bugs for transactions,
  mutation testing and fuzz targets.
  - A commit of unknown outcome inside a transaction is tested only against the
  fake (`TestConn_writeIn`).
  - The core's "can only be rolled back" state without a stop is not reachable
  on the real core from the engine's tests, because every real cache failure
  that is not "busy" stops the instance. Only the stop is tested.

  ### What a reviewer should look at

  - `Tx.RollbackTo` in internal/core/tx.go.
  - `leaveEnd` and `ending` in internal/engine/tx.go, the cut in `Rows.read`,
  `Conn.Close` and `pending` in internal/engine/engine.go.
  - The lease-lost test in `TestConn_stop`. It cuts off the client with
  `simstore.Client.CutOff` while a second instance takes the lease and commits,
  inside a synctest bubble.
  - The README prose, written by a writer sub-agent and reviewed by me, against
  the code.

  The `/code-review` pass at high found no blocking bug and six minor points.
  All six are fixed, and step 4 of the diary records them.

⏺ Transactions in the engine are built and in pull request #59. CI is running,
  and an independent reviewer is going through it before I merge, because the
  builder had to change the storage core.

  That change is the one thing worth knowing: to make a failed statement leave a
  transaction usable, as in SQLite, the core now has a savepoint around every
  write in a transaction. Without it, a statement cut off halfway (a lost lease,
  an ended context) could leave its row in a transaction that then commits. The
  reviewer is asked to try to make the commit object and the cache disagree.

  The rest works as decided: a writer from BeginTx, read-only on one state, one
  commit object per transaction. A write in a read-only transaction gives
  SQLite's "attempt to write a readonly database".

  The builder for the generated checks of parameters is still running. Usage is
  at 81% of the week.

✻ Crunched for 38s · done 3:03 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Verdict: sound to merge

  I found no case where the commit object and the cache disagree, no case where
  a commit is acknowledged without its changes in the log, and no case where the
  write connection is used twice at once. One liveness finding (1) should go
  into `docs/todo.md` or come to Markus as a design question. It does not block
  the merge.

  `go test ./internal/core/ ./internal/engine/ -short -count=1` passes in the
  worktree. I wrote two scratch test files there and removed them again; `git
  status` there is clean.

  ## Findings, most important first

  ### 1. An idle write transaction keeps a lost lease from being taken back
  (medium, liveness, proven)

  - **Where:** `internal/core/lease.go:323` (`acquire`) and `:432` (`resume`)
  both wait on `writerLock`. An open `core.Tx` holds that lock from `Begin`
  (`tx.go:84`) until `end` (`tx.go:622`).
  - **Sequence:**
    1. Instance a runs `Begin` and one insert, then sits idle.
    2. a is cut off from the store. b takes the lease, commits, and closes.
    3. a waits.
  - **Result:** with the transaction still open, `a.Wait` had not returned after
  24 hours of simulated time (`context deadline exceeded`). After `c.Rollback`,
  a served again at once.
  - **What is safe:** nothing commits that the spec forbids. `usable()` refuses
  the commit (`!holder` or `!serving`), and because the lease cannot be taken
  back while the transaction is open, a stale transaction can never become
  usable again.
  - **Cost:** the spec (line 44) says the instance "keeps trying to get the
  lease, catches up its cache when it gets it, and then serves again". An open
  transaction that the application leaves idle breaks that: after any lease
  blip, every read on the instance fails with `ErrNoLease` until the application
  ends the transaction. A single instance whose lease ran out by the clocks
  also needs `resume`, which needs the same lock, so it hits this too.
  - **Is it new?** The core always had this property. What is new is that the
  engine now hands long-lived transactions to applications. `database/sql`'s
  rollback when the context of `BeginTx` ends softens it only for callers who
  give a context with a deadline.
  - **Options:** the lease task could fail and roll back the open transaction
  when the lease is lost, or the behaviour could be documented. `docs/todo.md`
  does not mention it.

  ### 2. `core.Tx.RollbackTo` restores the cache and the transaction's record
  together (no fault found, proven by a randomized test)

  I wrote a scratch property test: 2,000 seeds, 6 transactions each, 25 random
  steps per transaction. The steps were:
  - `CreateTable` and `DropTable` on two names, in random upper or lower case;
  - `Upsert` and `Delete` on 4 keys;
  - `Savepoint`, `Release` and `RollbackTo` in random places, with any savepoint
  still open at the end rolled back.

  After every run it compared the committing instance's cache with a replay of
  the log into an empty cache. All 2,000 agreed. The runs covered:
  - drop, then create of the same name, with a rollback to a savepoint in
  between;
  - first touch of a table's key record inside the savepoint;
  - a drop inside the savepoint that removes a record which existed before it;
  - a statement that fails before it changes anything.

  Why it holds:
  - SQLite's savepoint also covers the `temp` schema, so the temporary tables of
  touched keys and their rows roll back with the main tables.
  - `savepoint.tables` copies every entry by value, and its fields (`created`,
  `dropped`, `touched`) are only ever replaced, never changed in place. The
  rebuilt map and order point at the same new copies.
  - `temps` is not restored, but it only counts up, so a temporary table's name
  is never reused.
  - `forget()` runs on both sides of `rollback to`.

  A second rollback, or a release with no savepoint, is refused with
  `ErrInvalid`. A rollback past an annotation fails the transaction. The engine
  cannot reach that case, because it annotates last.

  The test is worth keeping permanently: the core tests do not cover
  drop-then-create of one name across a savepoint.

  ### 3. Ending a transaction while rows are open (no fault found)

  - `Conn.Commit` of a write transaction while a loop reads its rows returns
  `ErrRowsOpen` and rolls back. `leaveEnd` returns true only for rows being
  read, and the first case of the switch catches those for write transactions,
  so no commit is acknowledged without its changes.
  - The cut in `Rows.read` is checked after every `yield`, so no row is read
  after the end. Leaving the range loop runs the core scan's `leave()` before
  `All`'s deferred `end` runs `ending.end`. So the rollback never meets an
  active statement, and the writer's place is freed when the loop ends, also
  after a panic.
  - While rows are pending, every statement, begin and `Close` on the connection
  is refused. Other connections and the lease task wait on `writerLock`.
  - The writer's place stays taken forever only if an `iter.Pull` over the rows
  is never stopped, which the docs state.

  ### 4. Read-only transactions (no fault found)

  The view is fixed when `Read` reads the cache's position, and every statement
  then uses the same SQLite read transaction. `Serving` is checked before each
  statement. A write only builds its sink, which looks up names, and never runs
  it, and `readOnly` writes nothing.

  ### 5. The tests (no timing dependence)

  Every wait in `tx_test.go` and the core `TestTx_Savepoint` runs inside
  `synctest`, under a fake clock. `TestConn_writeIn` checks the exact order of
  calls against a fake, so it would catch a `release` in place of `rollbackTo`.

  A gap: in slice 1 an insert has one row, and its unique check comes before its
  write. So from the engine, a rollback to a savepoint with a real change in it
  can only be reached through a stop or a lost lease between the upsert and the
  annotation, and no engine test against the real core goes down that path. The
  core tests and the property test in finding 2 cover `RollbackTo` itself.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Review transactions in engine" finished · 7m 8s

⏺ The review found the engine's transactions sound to merge: in 2,000 random
  runs with savepoints, the committing instance's cache always matched a replay
  of the log into an empty cache. CI is at 14 of 16 passed; I merge when the
  last two are in.

  It also found one thing that needs your decision.

  An open transaction that sits idle keeps an instance from taking its lease 
  back.

  If the instance loses its lease while a write transaction is open, the lease
  task cannot get the lease again until the application ends that transaction,
  because both need the writer's place. Until then every read on the instance
  fails. The reviewer showed it: 24 simulated hours without serving, and serving
  at once after the rollback.

  Nothing wrong can commit. The open transaction is already lost at that point:
  its commit is always refused once the lease has lapsed.

  My recommendation: when the lease is lost, the instance fails the open
  transaction at once. It is rolled back, the writer's place is freed, and its
  later statements and its commit give the "not serving" error. The application
  loses nothing it could still have had.

  The spec says today: "A write transaction that is already open commits only if
  its manifest swap still succeeds." I would change that to say that a commit
  that is under way goes on, and a transaction that is open but not committing
  fails. Agree with the behaviour? If yes, I'll propose the exact sentence.

⏺ Transactions in the engine are merged (#59, 16 of 16 checks passed, review

  ReadOnly: true, tx.Commit, tx.Rollback, and a new public error ErrTooLarge.

  Still running beside it: the builder for the generated checks of parameters.

  Waiting on you: when an instance loses its lease while a write transaction is
  open and idle, should the instance fail that transaction at once so that it
  can take the lease back? I recommend yes. Today such a transaction can never
  commit anyway, and it keeps the instance from serving until the application
  ends it.

  ## Report: transactions through `database/sql`

  Transactions now work through `database/sql`. The work is on branch
  `tx-driver`, which starts at `origin/main` `e8f5922` and has three commits
  (`4fb5d39`, `9c3e1c5`, `2f8a0b6`). Nothing is pushed.

  **Checks:** `make test` passes (exit 0), and `make lint` finds 0 issues. `make
  check-browser` and `go build -tags s4ql_browser ./...` pass.
  `internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck` passes with and without `-short`. The
  new tests and the ones I changed pass with `-count=20`, also under `-race`.
  The transaction tests also pass with `-count=50`, with and without `-race`,
  with two runs at the same time for load.

  ### What I built
  - **`/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a4eee22df4bf577fb
  /tx.go` (new):**
    - `conn.BeginTx` and `conn.Begin`.
    - The type `tx`, with `Commit` and `Rollback`.
    - `conn.ResetSession` and `conn.IsValid`.
    - `conn` has a new field `tx`.
  - **`errors.go`:**
    - A new public sentinel, `ErrTooLarge`, matched through `core.ErrTooLarge`.
    - The engine's `ErrRowsOpen` now maps to "not supported", with a message
  that says to close the rows first.
  - **`conn.go`:** the old `BeginTx`/`Begin` are gone, and the doc of `Close`
  changed.
  - **`s4ql.go`:** the package documentation has a new section "Transactions"
  with every point of item 7 of the brief, and `ErrTooLarge` is in the list of
  errors.
  - **Tests:**
    - `tx_test.go` (new) has `TestTx`, `TestTx_wait` (in synctest bubbles),
  `TestTx_databaseSQL`, `TestTx_instance`, and `TestTx_raw`. They cover every
  acceptance point. A test also reads the log to check that no argument value is
  in it.
    - `TestStop` now expects `ErrStopped` from `BeginTx`.
    - `TestConn_Raw` expects a working `Begin`.
    - `TestExec` checks that `begin`, `commit`, `end`, `rollback`, `savepoint`,
  and `release` as SQL text are refused, also inside a transaction.
    - `TestError` lists `ErrTooLarge` among the sentinels and has cases for the
  two new mappings.
    - In `s4ql_test.go`, `values` and `query` now take a `querier`, so they also
  work on a `sql.Tx` and a `sql.Conn`.
  - **`drivercheck_test.go`:** only the `begin` call changed. It begins on both
  sides, expects success on both, and rolls both back. Its pinned outcome is
  `begin`, and the tally requires `begin ok`. I also fixed one comment that had
  become untrue.
  - **Docs:**
    - `docs/roadmap.md` has a new section "Slice 3: transactions", and slice 1's
  status line now says which slice added parameters and which added `BeginTx`.
    - `docs/todo.md` has the updated lines and the driver's decisions.
    - The diary is `docs/diary/2026-11-19-transactions-driver.md`, with 2 steps.
    - A writer sub-agent wrote the roadmap and todo text from my list of
  decisions. I checked its diff against the code.

  ### Decisions of detail (all in `docs/todo.md` under "Transactions")
  1. **Context of the commit:** the commit uses the context given to `BeginTx`.
  `database/sql` passes the driver the application's own context, not the
  derived one that it cancels before it calls the driver's `Commit`. The roll
  back uses `context.WithoutCancel` of that context.
  2. **Isolation levels:** Default and Serializable are accepted for both kinds.
  Every other level gives `s4ql: not supported: the isolation level <name>`.
  This is checked before the instance, so a stopped instance gives the same
  error, and nothing is begun.
  3. **`ErrTooLarge`:** its message starts with `s4ql: the transaction is too
  large to commit: `, and it matches no other sentinel.
  4. **Open rows:** `ErrRowsOpen` is "not supported", with three messages:
     - A statement: `a statement while the rows of a query of a transaction on
  its connection are open; close the rows first`.
     - `BeginTx` on a `sql.Conn` whose rows are open: `...; close the rows
  first`.
     - A commit while a loop reads rows, which only the driver's own interface
  can do: `...so the transaction is rolled back and not committed`.
  5. **Second `BeginTx` on a `sql.Conn`:** `database/sql` allows it. It gets the
  plain error `s4ql: a transaction is open on the connection`, with no
  sentinel. Statements of a `sql.Conn` whose transaction is open run inside that
  transaction.
  6. **Ending the wrong transaction:** the driver tracks the open transaction. A
  `Commit` or `Rollback` of a `driver.Tx` that is not the open one gives `s4ql:
  internal error: the transaction has ended` and changes nothing.
  7. **`Rollback`:** it returns nil when the engine gives nil,
  `core.ErrStopped`, or `core.ErrNoLease`. Any other error, such as busy or
  internal, is returned.
  8. **Read-only commit:** it returns nil, also on a stopped instance or one
  that does not serve. This resolves an item that was open in todo.
  9. **Keeping a transaction out of the pool:** `IsValid` is false while a
  transaction is open, so `database/sql` closes the connection instead of
  pooling it, and the close rolls the transaction back. `ResetSession` resets
  nothing and returns nil, never `ErrBadConn`. Having both makes `database/sql`
  keep the connection after it rolls back because a context ended.
  10. **Deprecated `Begin`:** it begins a write transaction with
  `context.Background()`.
  11. **Telemetry:** no new events. `stop_seen` is logged through the existing
  path when `BeginTx` or a commit is answered with the stop.

  ### Differences from the brief
  - **Rollback can still return an error.** The brief says nil "whenever the
  transaction is ended and nothing is committed". I return nil for the stop and
  the lost lease, but not for other failures such as busy, so faults are not
  hidden.
  - **One branch of `Rollback` is untested.** A probe, deleted afterwards and
  described in the diary, tried to make the engine's own roll back fail after a
  local stop. Every kind of `faultvfs` failure left the roll back touching no
  file, so no test reaches that branch.
  - **No change to `internal/core`, `internal/engine`, or
  `internal/simulator`.**

  ### What is left
  - Generated comparisons of transactions through `database/sql` (`stmttest`,
  `drivercheck`).
  - A simulation workload with transactions, planted bugs, mutation testing, and
  fuzz targets.
  - The open question for the product owner, which the writer recorded in todo
  because no document had it: an idle write transaction keeps an instance that
  lost its lease from taking it back. I confirmed that the lease task takes the
  core's `writerLock`.

  ### What a reviewer should look at
  - **Code review findings.** `/code-review high` found five nits, none
  reachable through `database/sql`. I fixed three (the `ErrRowsOpen` message,
  `ErrTooLarge` in the sentinel lists, a doc wrap). Two are left and written in
  todo, both reachable only through `sql.Conn.Raw`:
    - After a commit or roll back while a loop reads rows, `IsValid` is true
  while the engine still holds the writer's place.
    - The engine's `Conn.Close` gives `ErrReaderInUse` and rolls nothing back
  while a loop reads. This one predates the branch.
  - **`tx.Rollback`'s error filter**, and the `WithoutCancel`. A mutant that
  drops `WithoutCancel` survives, because the core uses the roll back's context
  only for events.
  - **Hangs instead of failures.** Some of my hand mutants were killed only by
  the one-minute test timeout, not by an assertion: a write transaction that
  should not exist makes a later begin wait forever.
  - **A possible data race to watch.** If `db.Close()` shuts the open rows of a
  transaction while the application is running a statement on that same
  transaction, two goroutines touch the engine's row state. The driver already
  had this pattern for rows outside transactions. My load test does not make
  that overlap.
  - **The writer's prose** in `docs/roadmap.md` (slice 3) and in the driver's
  list in `docs/todo.md`.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  ## Report: branch `tx-checks-driver`

  All three parts are done, and the two faults that turned up in the merged code
  are fixed. The branch has six commits on `origin/main` at `0818823`. I did
  not push it.

  **Checks:** all exit 0:
  - `make test`, `make lint`, `make check-browser`, and `go build -tags
  s4ql_browser ./...`.
  - `go test -short -race -count=20 .` (123 s).
  - `go test -race -count=20 ./drivercheck` in `internal/sqlite/crosscheck` (211
  s), and the same with `-count=20` without the race detector.
  - `FuzzSequence` for 30 s (5,482 inputs).

  ### 1. Generated transactions in `drivercheck`

  **What mattn does:** a probe showed it ignores `ReadOnly: true` (an insert
  goes through) and accepts every isolation level, including
  `IsolationLevel(99)`. So the oracle is opened with `?_txlock=immediate`: its
  transactions then take the writer's place when they begin, as s4ql's do. A
  read-only transaction runs under `pragma query_only = 1`. The new `TestPair`
  checks that the oracle's begin takes the lock, and it fails with
  `_txlock=deferred`.

  **How it works:** a transaction is now state of the pair, open on both sides.
  - `begin` and `begin read-only` take the isolation level from their text.
  `commit` and `rollback` end the transaction, and the outcome of each end and
  every table after it are compared.
  - New calls:
    - `prepare outside, exec/query in the transaction` prepares on the `DB` or
  `Conn` and runs through `tx.StmtContext`.
    - `query, rows left open` leaves rows open for the next call.
    - `query beside the transaction` runs on a pinned `Conn` (inside the
  transaction) or, through the pool, on the `DB` outside it.
  - They are appended to `calls`, so old fuzz bytes keep their meaning.
  - Through the pool, a begin while a transaction is open commits that one
  first, and a non-select "beside" call runs inside the transaction. Both avoid
  a deadlock: a second writer would wait for the first.

  **Numbers:** 200 sequences make 499 transactions:
  - 342 write: 240 commit, 102 roll back.
  - 157 read-only: 119 commit, 38 roll back.
  - On pinned connections and through the pool.

  `TestGenerated` requires 60 more outcome kinds, covering every bullet of the
  brief.

  **Pinned counts.** These counts are of calls; the numbers before this branch
  are in brackets.

  | Pin | Count | What it counts |
  |---|---|---|
  | `excusedPrepare` | 1051 (1120) | SQLite fails at prepare, s4ql when the
  statement runs |
  | `excusedStartedTable` | 12 (13) | a create table that SQLite finds started |
  | `excusedArguments` | 364 (412) | wrong number of arguments, checked against
  s4ql's rule |
  | `excusedArgument` | 109 (103) | an argument s4ql does not support, checked
  against its rule |
  | `excusedLevel` (new) | 67 | an isolation level s4ql refuses; not run in
  SQLite |
  | `excusedRowsOpen` (new) | 44 | a statement while rows of its transaction are
  open; must give s4ql's message, not run in SQLite |
  | `excusedTxOpen` (new) | 28 | a second begin on a pinned connection; s4ql
  must give `s4ql: a transaction is open on the connection` (no sentinel),
  SQLite its own refusal |
  | `excusedOutside` (new) | 43 | see below |

  `excusedOutside` covers one case: through the pool, mattn's Prepare outside
  the transaction refuses a name that only the transaction has. It is then
  compared with mattn's statement prepared inside the transaction, which is
  where database/sql prepares it again.

  - The old four moved only because `pick` draws other random numbers. Each is
  explained in the test's comment and the commit message.
  - Six new hand-written sequences cover: a commit, a roll back with create and
  drop inside, a read-only transaction, rows left open, isolation levels and a
  second begin, and a query beside the transaction.
  - Two mutants of `tx.go` by hand were caught: `Rollback` calling `Commit`, and
  `ReadOnly` ignored.

  ### 2. The race: real, and fixed

  The new `closeBeside` helper in `conn_test.go` closes a fresh database 20
  times while another goroutine runs statements on the connection whose rows are
  open. It is used once in a transaction (`TestTx_instance`) and once outside
  one, on a `sql.Conn` (`TestClose`); no existing test made that overlap. Both
  reported `DATA RACE` under `-race`: `engine.(*Rows).end` wrote what
  `engine.(*Conn).rowsOpen` read. One side was `connector.Close`/`rows.shut` (or
  the loop's task); the other was `conn.ExecContext`.

  **The fix is in the driver only** (`conn.go`, `rows.go`, `tx.go`):
  - Every call that reaches the engine holds the engine connection from
  `conn.enter` to `conn.leave`, and `rows.shut` holds it too.
  - If a call holds it, `shut` leaves the rows to that call, which closes them
  when it ends. So `db.Close()` never waits for a call.
  - A plain mutex would not do: a write waiting for the writer's place wakes
  only at the stop of the instance, and the close makes that stop after closing
  the rows, so `db.Close()` could hang.
  - simlint forbids `sync.Cond`, so the hand-over uses a host semaphore.

  **Cost:** a small mutex taken twice per call, uncontended because database/sql
  makes one call per connection at a time. A call waits only if it arrives
  while the connector is closing that connection's rows.

  A temporary probe showed both branches run: of 180 closes, 167 and 174 (two
  runs) left the rows to a running call, and 13 and 6 closed them at once. One
  branch is not shown by any test: a call that arrives while `shut` is closing
  rows and waits for the hand-over.

  ### 3. The two `sql.Conn.Raw` cases: both fixed

  They are one fix. Fixing only `IsValid` would make database/sql close such
  connections, and the engine's close, refused with `ErrReaderInUse`, would then
  leave their reader and transaction to no one.
  - The connection counts its open rows (`held`). `IsValid` is false while a
  transaction is open or rows are open.
  - When the engine's `Close` gives `core.ErrReaderInUse` while rows are open,
  `conn.Close` sets `closeLater` and returns nil. The close of the last of those
  rows then closes the engine connection, which closes the reader and rolls
  back a transaction that is still open. The error of that close becomes the
  error of the rows' `Close`.
  - The new `TestTx_raw` case runs after a commit, after a roll back, and with
  the transaction still open. It failed before the fix and passes now. Normal
  database/sql use is unchanged.

  ### Docs

  - **`docs/roadmap.md`:** only the sentence about the generated comparison
  through `database/sql`, now with the numbers above.
  - **`docs/todo.md`:**
    - The "Known, through the driver's own interfaces only" entry is now the
  decision about `IsValid` and the deferred close.
    - A new entry records the race fix.
    - The entry on `drivercheck`'s `begin` now describes the transactions.
    - "Generated comparisons of transactions through `database/sql`" is deleted
  from "Not built".
  - I left "Mutation testing and fuzz targets over transactions" alone because
  the other builder owns it. `FuzzSequence` now makes transactions, so that
  entry may want to say so.

  ### Differences from the brief

  - The diary is at `docs/diary/2026-11-20-transactions-checks-driver.md` as you
  named it, but the commits and the two new todo decisions are dated
  2026-11-19, which is the date here.
  - I changed no file in `internal/engine` or `internal/core`.

  ### What to look at

  - `conn.enter`, `conn.leave`, `conn.freeLocked`, and `rows.shut`: the
  hand-over, and that nothing waits for the connection while holding `rows.mu`.
  - `closeLater` and `held` in `conn.Close` and `rows.close`, and the new
  `IsValid` in `tx.go`.
  - In `drivercheck_test.go`: `excusedOutside` in `compare`, and `pair.where`
  for the "beside" call through the pool.
  - The fuzz run shows bursts with seconds at `0/sec` between them. The file
  from `main` shows the same at a machine load of about 15, so it is not this
  change.

⏺ Agent "Simulation and mutation of transactions" is waiting on its own

⏺ Agent "Simulation and mutation of transactions" is waiting on its own
  background work · 1h 1m 55s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Verdict: sound to merge.** I found no deadlock, hang or race that can happen
  through `database/sql`. The new tests catch the race they were written for.
  The two findings below are hardening and a gap in the tests, not blockers.

  **What I checked, and how**
  - **The tests catch the race.** In the worktree I put `conn.go`, `rows.go` and
  `tx.go` back to their `origin/main` versions and ran the two new
  `closeBeside` subtests under `-race -count=3`. Both failed with 5 DATA RACE
  reports. I then restored the files.
  - **Stress run.** The new subtests of `TestClose`, `TestTx_instance` and
  `TestTx_raw` pass at `-race -count=40`. The wider run `-race -count=3 -run
  'TestClose|TestTx'` passes too.
  - **Writer wait during the close (your point 1), proved with a scratch test,
  now removed.** Another connection holds a write transaction. Connection A has
  rows open, read either up to their first row or not at all. On A, an insert
  waits for the writer's place, and then `db.Close()` runs. This repeated 10
  times under `-race` and never hung. The rows' close is left to the insert.
  `core.DB.Close` stops the instance first, and `acquireServing` wakes on the
  stop. The insert fails with "stopped: closed", its deferred `leave` closes the
  rows, and `waitClosed` ends.
  - **Lock order is acyclic.**
    - `shut` and `Rows.Close` take `rows.mu` and then `conn.mu`.
    - `leave` lets go of `conn.mu` before it takes `rows.mu`.
    - In `conn.Close`, the deferred `c.mu.Unlock` runs before the deferred
  `leave`, because defers run last in, first out.
    - Nothing holds `rows.mu` and then calls `enter`, and `shut` never waits for
  a call.
  - **The hand-over to a waiting call does run.** A coverage run showed that
  `enter`'s waiting branch (conn.go:238) and the hand-over in `freeLocked`
  (conn.go:269) both ran, with no hang.
  - **No path reaches the engine outside the hold (point 2).**
    - `Ping` calls only `db.Wait`, and `ResetSession` does nothing.
    - Prepared statements, the legacy `Stmt.Exec` and `Raw` all go through
  `ExecContext`, `QueryContext` and `BeginTx`.
    - `IsValid` reads `held` under `mu`. `Commit` and `Rollback` write `c.tx`
  without `mu`, but `database/sql` holds the driverConn lock around those calls
  and around `IsValid`.
    - `Next` and `Rows.Close` stay outside the hold. `database/sql` makes them
  under the driverConn lock, so they are ordered against statements on the
  connection, and `rows.mu` orders them against `shut`.
  - **Rows left to a running call (point 3).** `leave` is deferred, so it closes
  them also when the call fails or panics. Through `database/sql`, a `Next`
  after `db.Close()` needs the driverConn lock, so it comes after that close and
  gives errClosed.
  - **`closeLater` (point 4).** It is cleared once, under `mu`, and the engine's
  `Close` does nothing on a second call. If an application never closes driver
  rows it took through `Raw`, the transaction keeps the writer's place until
  `db.Close()` closes those rows. That is misuse of the driver's own interface,
  and better than on `main`, where that transaction leaked forever.

  **Findings, by importance**

  1. **A panic in `r.close` locks the connection forever.** Low likelihood: it
  needs an engine bug.
     - Where: `conn.go` `leave` (about lines 250–258) and `rows.go` `shut`
  (about lines 199–203).
     - What happens: in `leave`, the `r.mu.Unlock()` after `r.close()` is not
  deferred, and neither is the final `freeLocked`. The same is true of the
  `freeLocked` after `_ = r.close()` in `shut`. If `r.close` panics, `busy`
  stays true. A panic can come from `engine.(*Rows).Close` → `end` →
  `report.panicked`, or from the engine's `Close` on the `closeLater` path.
     - Interleaving: `db.Close()` leaves rows to a running statement. The
  statement's `leave` calls `r.close`, which panics, and the application
  recovers. `database/sql` later calls `conn.Close`. Its `enter` sets `waiting`
  and waits on `turn` forever. A close of the same rows from `database/sql`'s
  own goroutine also hangs, because `rows.mu` is still held.
     - On `main`, the same panic only propagated. Here it also leaves the
  connection hung.
     - Fix: use `defer r.mu.Unlock()` inside a small closure in `leave`, and
  defer the free in `shut`.
     - I reasoned this out and did not prove it with a test. The engine has no
  planted bug that panics in `Rows.Close`.

  2. **No test forces the hand-over branch.** Gap in the tests only.
     - The branch where a call arrives while `shut` is closing rows (`enter`
  waits, `freeLocked` releases `turn`) runs only when the timing happens to
  allow it.
     - Pass or fail does not depend on timing: the race detector flags unordered
  accesses wherever they fall, and no test sleeps. Only the coverage of this
  branch depends on timing.
     - A test that forces it would hold `shut` inside `r.close`, for example by
  letting a hook block the rows' task, while a statement enters.

  The tests do not depend on timing. `TestTx_raw` bounds only from the side a
  slow machine cannot break, with a one-minute timeout. It uses the driver
  connection after `Raw` returns, which is on purpose, to exercise the driver's
  own interfaces.

  The worktree at
  /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a264eb12270df3248 is
  clean: I removed the scratch test and the coverage file, and `git status`
  shows nothing.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
